THE WHOLE OF WHAT WENT OUT IS ONE PENNY, FOR A SHEET OF PAPER AT A COUNTER OFF THE FISH MARKET ON THE **TWENTY-EIGHTH** OF JANUARY, AT ABOUT ELEVEN ON THAT THURSDAY MORNING, THE FIRST MONEY THIS YARD HAS SPENT SINCE THE EIGHTEENTH OF DECEMBER; **THE FIGURE OF THE TWENTY-SEVENTH IS WITHDRAWN.** THREE WAGES OF FOUR MARKS WERE NOT PAID, on Tuesday the nineteenth and twenty-sixth of January and Tuesday the second of February, no envelope was made on any of them, and the number of weeks fallen due with no envelope standing against them is TWELVE at the open, THIRTEEN after the nineteenth of January, FOURTEEN after the twenty-sixth and FIFTEEN after the second of February, all fifteen given in the same breath wherever the figure appears. THE BAG WAS 1,996p SHORT OF THE WAGE AT THE OPEN AND 4,395p SHORT AT THE CLOSE, A RISE OF 2,399p, **AND IT GOT WORSE ON FIVE DAYS AND ONE PENNY AND EVERY ONE OF THE FIVE IS A DAY THIS YARD WORKED; THE FIGURES *3,915p*, *SIX DAYS* AND *EVERY ONE OF THE SIX* ARE WITHDRAWN.**

**THE CALENDAR IS THE VOLUME'S OWN GRID AND THE CHAPTERS DECIDE IT: Friday the fifteenth of January to Wednesday the third of February, TWENTY CONSECUTIVE DAYS, TWO DAYS TO A CHAPTER, NO DAY MISSING AND NO DAY IN TWO CHAPTERS, NO COST PAID TWICE, NO YEAR STATED AND NO WEEKDAY STATED THAT THE RULE DOES NOT GIVE.** The first of December 2026 is a Tuesday and every day follows from that and from the chapter before it. The wage Tuesdays are the nineteenth and twenty-sixth of January and the second of February, in Chapters 223, 226 and 230. The Mondays are the eighteenth and twenty-fifth of January and the first of February, in Chapters 222, 226 and 229, and the slate-wall wipes are the **thirty-seventh, thirty-eighth and thirty-ninth**, all bare, the counter not restarted, the clerk keeping his own page. The rota is a Monday and a Thursday, so the rota days are the twenty-first and twenty-eighth of January. **THE HEARING ROOM WAS NOT USED ON ANY DAY OF THIS BATCH AND THE MARKET WALL WAS NOT TOUCHED ON ANY DAY OF IT AND THE MARKET OFFICE WAS NOT USED; a copyist's counter off the fish market was used once, on the twenty-eighth, for one sheet of paper at a penny; the head of this dock was used once, on the sixteenth. NO SHEET WENT UP ON ANY WALL IN THIS HARBOR IN THE TWENTY DAYS ENDED ON THE THIRD OF FEBRUARY AND NONE CAME OFF. THE CISTERN WAS CHARGED ON FIVE ROTA DAYS AND WALKED ON NONE, BEING THE EIGHTEENTH, TWENTY-FIRST, TWENTY-FIFTH AND TWENTY-EIGHTH DAYS OF JANUARY AND THE FIRST OF FEBRUARY, AND THE NEXT ROTA DAY IS THE FOURTH OF FEBRUARY AND IS PAST THE END OF THIS BATCH. THE FAR BENCH WAS OCCUPIED ON ONE DAY AND ON NO OTHER, BEING MONDAY THE FIRST OF FEBRUARY, AND NEITHER BROTHER WENT OVER ON ANY OF THE TWENTY.**

**THE DUPLICATION AUDIT WAS RUN IN THREE PASSES OVER THE TEN CHAPTERS AND ALL FIFTY CHAPTERS OF VOLUME 04 AND ALL TEN CHAPTERS OF VOLUME 05 BATCH 0001 TOGETHER, BEING SEVENTY FILES, AND THE METHOD IS STATED: every paragraph of twelve words or more compared byte for byte, SIX byte-identical duplicates found and SIX repaired in place, first instance left, **ONE** at the final pass and that one the ruled working day's board; the same set keyed on its first forty words and then run pairwise for proper containment, ELEVEN forty-word opening matches on the first run, SEVEN of them those same paragraphs and FOUR more repaired in place, first instance left, **FIVE** remaining and all five pre-existing and ruled, and TWO proper containment pairs, both pre-existing, neither involving a chapter of this batch; and every exact sentence of eight words or more, **ZERO recurring inside this batch on the first run and ZERO against the twenty chapters of the volume written before it.** The detail is `state/continuity.md` § 10 of the Batch 0003 section and the reason the one remaining duplicate is not repaired is the Volume 04 audit's § 21d: **a harbor that improves on its own phrasing is a different book.**

**THE ONE PHASE THIS BATCH CREATED IS `workspace/volume-05/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`, THE VOLUME 05 BATCH 0004 WRITER PROMPT FOR CHAPTERS 231 TO 240, MOVEMENT 4. NOTHING ELSE WAS CREATED: no batch-0005 prompt, no Volume 05 close prompt, no Volume 06 anything, no `.done` marker and no `.retired` marker. The volume's chapter range is 201 to 250 and this batch carries it to 230, so the volume is not closed, and the volume's final chapter image is not reached and was not attempted and is the close's work and not a batch's.**

### Chapter 221 — Four Ways a Page Comes Up a Hill, and a Line With No Name In It
Friday the fifteenth and Saturday the sixteenth of January, in the shed, and at the boards. **No day of work booked on either day, and the reason against the line is that a day on which nothing happened still happened and that a line of that book has been read out in a market and in a room in about four months and has never once been read out in that shed. The far bench is entered as not occupied on both days with no figure of what came before it.** **THE CHAPTER'S WORK IS A READING OF TWO SECOND-COLUMN LINES AND A QUESTION:** a line of places kept by more than one community for longer than any book in that office goes back; and a record of a chain of vessels kept by nine communities with a date against each and a place against each, said out loud at a weir in a month this party is not going to name. **The bare fact of the nine was already in that book and is not new in this volume, and she says so, and the answer to *what are the nine* is that she does not know and has got nothing behind it, and that the number nine is in four different places in this book and not one of the four is a thing they could walk to. THE FOUR ARRIVE BY FOUR ROUTES AND THE FOUR ROUTES ARE THE CHAPTER: SHOWN — a man of sixty-four of the first lay, a joiner from a place four days up this coast, who has a book of his own begun at seventeen and has never shown it to anybody, standing up at the boards, who says if you had asked for the book I would have said no, and that he will bring one leaf up on the Monday and take it down on the Tuesday, and who says he has carried it nine years and in nine years has never once asked himself whether he is allowed and has asked instead whether anybody has noticed; PRODUCED — a woman of seventy-one who has kept a page in a house for forty years and has never been asked for it, who walks up that mole and says she is not answering a knock and that there is a man in the market putting the word nine into a conversation about a page, and who says at half past nine that one of them is going to ask her a question about a night and she knew at half past seven and did not let herself stop walking; ORDINARY TRAFFIC — a man of about thirty-four who sells lime and chalk, sixty pounds short, who found a folded sheet in a crate of chalk on the Tuesday boat and carried it up that hill on nothing and is going to be asked about it at four houses this week; AND A BOAT'S LOCKER, said in nine words at the head of that dock by the man of the second lay on his way to get the pump. No record is opened and the chapter says why: a page is not read on the day it arrives. No new institution, no new antagonist, and no office handed one over. **THE COST IS IN THE SECOND COLUMN: this party went to four households on two days and asked to see a page, and that is the same act at a smaller size as the refusal of the tenth of December, and a man in this town can now come up that hill and ask any one of the four of them whether this party came, and be told that it did.** **No money in and nothing out on either day; 2,636p in the bag, 1,996p short of the wage, twelve weeks fallen due with no envelope standing against them.**

### Chapter 222 — A Page That Is Not a Copy of Anything, and the Thirty-Seventh Monday
Sunday the seventeenth and Monday the eighteenth of January, in the shed, and on the face of the second seam. **THE WALL COMES DOWN BARE FOR THE THIRTY-SEVENTH MONDAY SINCE THE FIRST OF MAY, THE COUNTER DID NOT RESTART, THE CLERK KEPT HIS OWN PAGE, AND NO SITTING WAS HELD. THE CISTERN WAS CHARGED ON THE ROTA DAY AND WALKED ON NONE, AND THAT IS THE FIRST OF FIVE IN THIS MONTH.** The far bench is entered as not occupied on both days with no figure. **On the Sunday night the ship's hand did not come down at half past eight and did not come down at nine, and that is entered in the second column with a reason against the line and no name in it, and the reason is that a day this yard did not work is entered as a day this yard did not work and is not entered twice.** A working day on the Monday, on a hirer's own account, the pump and the man his, a hand lead's line over the side and back four times. **THE MAN OF THE SECOND LAY BRINGS A PAGE UP THE HILL IN A PIECE OF OILCLOTH AND SAYS ON HIS WAY DOWN THAT HE HAS NOT LOOKED INSIDE IT AND DOES NOT WANT TO. THE FIRST OF THE FOUR IS ONE SHEET OF RULED PAPER WITH THE FOLD AT THE LEFT EDGE A TEAR AND NOT A STITCH, TORN OUT OF SOMETHING THAT WAS NEVER BOUND AND CARRIED FOUR HUNDRED MILES IN A BOAT'S LOCKER, AND IT IS A PAGE THAT IS NOT A COPY OF ANYTHING AND IS NOT A DOCUMENT ANYBODY SENT ANYWHERE AND IS SIMPLY THE PAGE A PLACE HAS AT THE INSIDE OF A BAR, IN THREE HANDS, THE THIRD FOUR YEARS OLD AND SIGNED AT THE FOOT BECAUSE A BOSUN WRITES HIS NAME.** The ship's hand reads it and the holder does not read it for an hour and a quarter. **The line she reads out loud: the ninth, and a hard night, and the light on the point was burning, and two boats came in on it about two in the morning and lay there till it was light. She read it twice, stopped the second time and came back to the beginning, and would not say why, and it went in the second column with no name in it because a name would let a man tell her that a woman of seventy-one was in that shed on a Saturday morning.** The read-back runs. **Three marks in on the Monday and nothing out on either day; 3,116p in the bag, 2,476p short of the wage.**

### Chapter 223 — A Wage Tuesday, and a Line of a Page That Says the Light Was Out
Tuesday the nineteenth and Wednesday the twentieth of January, in the shed, and on the north side of this town. **THE WAGE DOES NOT GO IN, NO ENVELOPE IS MADE, AND THE NUMBER OF WEEKS FALLEN DUE IS THIRTEEN, all thirteen given in the same breath, and the reason is the reason of the twenty-ninth of December past and is not improved on.** No day of work on the Tuesday and a working day on the Wednesday. The two of them sit in that shed all Tuesday with a sheet of paper on the bench that does not belong to them and do not read one line of it, and the reason is that this yard has had two Tuesdays in a row where it did not work and there is nothing to say about that except that it happened. On the Tuesday afternoon the woman's page says that one of them is going to ask her about the ninth and that she has known it since half past seven that morning. **On the Wednesday they go down the hill to her front room, and she opens the door before they get to it and says that is not a courtesy, and he says that the page on his bench says a light was burning on the ninth and asks her what hers says, and she says it without looking at it: the ninth, and a hard night, and there was no light on the point, and the man that keeps it had the lamp out the week before and the oil was not carried, and the two boats that came in were in before it was light and were not in on the light. SHE WAS AT A WINDOW TWO HUNDRED YARDS OFF THE WATER AND THE POINT IS A MILE AND A HALF FROM THE OTHER PLACE AND SHE STOPPED TRYING TO MAKE THAT LINE SAY ANYTHING ELSE AT NINE YEARS OLD. ONE OF THEM IS WRONG. SHE IS NOT GOING TO SAY WHICH ONE AND NEITHER IS HE, AND SHE HAS KNOWN SINCE SHE WAS NINE.** **THE FINDING, IN HIS OWN WORDS AND IN THE SECOND COLUMN: there are two pages, there is a night, there is a point with a light on it or there is not, and neither of the two says where the person was standing when it was written, and that is not a thing about a light, it is a thing about what a page is.** She also reads a line about a sale of the right to take what came ashore, in words and not in figures, **and the words do not add up to the figures**, and says the page went out of that house twice in her lifetime to men from a city and came back twice. **THE COST: this yard went into a house on the north side of this town and asked a woman of seventy-one one question, and that is the first time in forty years anybody has asked that house anything, and the question is going to be in three other front rooms by Friday, and this yard cannot unask it and is not going to try.** **Three marks in on the Wednesday; 3,596p in the bag, 2,956p short of the wage.**

### Chapter 224 — A Stair in a House Four Days Up This Coast, and a Day of a Trade
Thursday the twenty-first and Friday the twenty-second of January, in the shed, and on the face of the second seam. **THE CISTERN WAS CHARGED ON THE ROTA DAY AND WALKED ON NONE, AND THAT IS THE SECOND OF FIVE. THE FAR BENCH IS ENTERED AS NOT OCCUPIED ON BOTH DAYS WITH NO FIGURE.** A man of sixty-four came up on the Monday and waited forty feet off the mole until the yard was in the water, and came back on the Thursday at about eight, and gives the reason: he is not going to stand on a mole at eight in the morning holding a book while a day is being worked. **He puts the fourth page of the ninth leaf on the bench himself, does not take his hand off it, says it will be the fourth page of the ninth leaf when it goes back in, and reads the line himself and will not have it read, because he has read it about forty times and wants to hear somebody else in the room while he does it. THE THIRD RECORD IS ABOUT ONE PERSON AND IS THE ONLY ONE OF THE FOUR THAT IS, AND SAYS: on a Tuesday in a bad month, a man of that place, aged thirty-one, went out in a boat of about sixty tons, and the tide was making at about six in the evening, and he did not come back, and there was a lantern in his pocket and a boot knife and two shillings and sixpence, and his mother held his things and his brother held the boat, and the boat came back and he did not.** And he says, before anybody else does, that a boat of about sixty tons in his page and a boat of about sixty tons in the locker page are two boats and he is not going to let two men make them one. **Then he asks the only question he came up the hill with: tell me whether I have been carrying it right. And the holder takes about nine seconds and says he is not going to tell him that, because he could not do it if he wanted to and he has watched a man refuse to say a thing he did not know and the whole of what this yard is not saying it. What he knows is that a page about a man with a lantern and a boot knife and two shillings and sixpence in it is worth a day. What he does not know is whether a page of a place has ever been carried out of that place, and he has never met anybody who knows and has not got the name of anything in this country the answer would be in. And the man says he will go and set a stair and put the page back where it was and go over the same four yards of road looking at the same thing, and the holder says he is going to keep doing it, and the man says he is sixty-four and it is the only thing he has that is not in a house where it does not belong. THE RECORD IS SHOWN AND LENT AND NOT READ.** A working day on the Friday. **THE COST: a two-day stair booked for the Thursday and done on the Friday and the Saturday, and a day of a joiner's trade at about three marks put by the man in his own book against the Thursday, entered by the yard because a man in this town can go and ask him, and entered also as not in this yard's money and not going to be.** **No money in and nothing out on either day; 3,596p in the bag.**

### Chapter 225 — A Crate of Chalk, and a Name at the Head of a Sheet
Saturday the twenty-third and Sunday the twenty-fourth of January, in the shed. **No day of work on either day, and the reason is that a day on which nothing happened still happened, and that these two days were two of the six days this fortnight that this party spent on somebody else's page. THE FAR BENCH IS NOT OCCUPIED ON EITHER DAY WITH NO FIGURE. AND ON NEITHER DAY WAS ANYTHING READ OUT IN A ROOM, NOTHING WAS READ OUT IN A MARKET, THERE WAS NO CLERK IN THAT SHED AND NO MAN WITH A SEAL IN THAT SHED AND NO OFFICE IN THAT SHED OF ANY KIND, and the reason is on the page.** The sheet comes out from under the pump in two pieces because the fold has gone through, and is put back together with a weight on the corner and the two pieces are not joined and are not joined now. **THE FOURTH OF THE FOUR IS A COPY, AND THE YARD KNOWS IT IS A COPY BECAUSE THE WRITING IS EVEN ALL THE WAY DOWN THE PAGE AND BECAUSE THE INK ON THE UPPER TWO THIRDS IS A DIFFERENT MAKE FROM THE INK ON THE LOWER THIRD, and there is no date on it and no number and no seal and no stamp and no signature and no office named anywhere on it. THE SHEIPWRIGHT MAKES THE HOLDER READ IT, TWICE, OUT LOUD, THE SAME WAY BOTH TIMES INCLUDING THE PARTS HE DOES NOT UNDERSTAND. AT THE HEAD OF IT, IN A HAND BIGGER THAN THE REST, IS A NAME, AND THE NAME IS WRITTEN ONCE IN THIS CHAPTER AND NOWHERE ELSE IN THE WHOLE OF VOLUME 05; under it, an agreement made at a place, between nine, concerning a page. Four lines are legible and two are not, and they say that a page about a place is the place's, and that it is not to be carried out of the place it is about, and that a copy is not a page and has none of a page's standing, and that no one of the nine is to be asked what another of the nine keeps. AT THE FOOT, IN A SECOND INK AND A LATER HAND OF THAT SAME PLACE, A LINE THAT IS NOT ONE OF THE FOUR: that a page was carried out of that place in a year nobody wrote down and was brought back in another year and has been kept ever since in a house that is not the house it was kept in before. IT DOES NOT SAY THE CARRYING WAS WRONG. THE SHEET DOES NOT SAY THE AGREEMENT HELD AND THE YARD IS NOT GOING TO SAY IT.** Three things are entered and not improved on: that the name is the name of an agreement and not of a body and nothing on the sheet says nine places made one thing of themselves; that the sheet is a copy made by a person who is not of that place; and **that the name is not going to be written in this yard's book, because a word said out loud in a shed to one other person is a word that exists in two people and not in a page.** On the Sunday afternoon the man of sixty-four puts his head in the door, looks at the four pages, does not come in, and says he is going to be asked at his own house by his aunt why he gave a page to two men he met on a mole, and if you were me you would say the truth, and he has never in his life said the truth to his aunt about anything. **The cost of that is in the second column with no name in it, and the reason against the line is that a man who has been made a reason without knowing it does not need a page.** **No money in and nothing out; 3,596p in the bag.**

### Chapter 226 — Two Hours at the Top of a Bench, and a Fifth Thing None of the Four Says
Monday the twenty-fifth and Tuesday the twenty-sixth of January, in the shed. **THE WALL COMES DOWN BARE FOR THE THIRTY-EIGHTH MONDAY, THE COUNTER DID NOT RESTART, THE CLERK KEPT HIS OWN PAGE, AND NO SITTING WAS HELD. THE CISTERN WAS CHARGED AND WALKED ON NONE, THE THIRD OF FIVE. THE FAR BENCH IS NOT OCCUPIED ON EITHER DAY WITH NO FIGURE.** A man of sixty-four brings the leaf at eight and takes the book away and will not be in the room, and gives the reason: a page read in a room with the person who keeps it in it is a page read to a person, and a thing read to a person is a thing that person has agreed with by being quiet, and he has been in a room where a man was quiet for an hour and a half and it was put in a book afterwards as though he had been asked. **A woman of seventy-one sends word by a boy of about eleven who was paid a penny out of a box at the top of that mole and not out of this yard's money: she will not be in the room, she will come on the Tuesday at about four for her page and will not stop, she will not be told about the reading afterwards, and she does not want a copy, and there is a piece of paper on that bench that says a copy is not a page, and she has never been asked anything about it and is not going to start. THE HOLDER ENTERS THAT THE WHOLE OF THAT IS TO HIS CREDIT AND NOT HERS.** **THE FOUR ARE READ SIDE BY SIDE FROM ABOUT TWO UNTIL ABOUT FOUR, AT THE TOP OF THAT BENCH, IN THAT SHED, IN ABOUT TWO HOURS, BY TWO PEOPLE, AND THERE WAS NO CLERK IN THAT ROOM AND NO MAN WITH A SEAL IN THAT ROOM AND NO MARKET ANYWHERE NEAR IT. THE FOUR ARE INCOMPATIBLE AND ALL FOUR OF THEM ARE TRUE, AND THE INCOMPATIBILITY IS THE FINDING. Two pages about one night and a light; a sale of the right to take what came ashore; a man who went out and did not come back; an agreement about a page. A sentence that would settle the light is that the two pages cannot both be of the same night, and that is true, and it does not say that one of them is a lie and it does not say that one of them is about a different night, and it says that either way the thing that is wrong is in that shed and not at either of the two places, and it was written four times in the holder's head and not put on the page. A boat of about sixty tons is in two of them and this yard is not going to make them into one. And the fourth, which is where it does the most and says the least: a copy is not a page, and that sentence is written on a copy; a page is not to be carried out of its place, and a man is going to collect one on the Tuesday and carry it four days up this coast; and at its foot, in a later hand of that same place, a page was carried out of that place in a year nobody wrote down, and the fourth says the carrying is not to happen and its own foot says it happened and does not say it was wrong.** A third person in that room would have made it checkable, and a third person in that room is a person who can be asked about it afterwards by a man with a seal. **THE FIFTH THING, WHICH IS ABOUT THE SHAPE OF THE FOUR AND IS NOT WHAT ANY OF THE FOUR SAYS: THERE IS NOT ONE DATE ON ANY OF THE FOUR, AND A PENCIL WAS OUT FOR TEN MINUTES AND FOUR DATES COULD HAVE BEEN INVENTED AND WOULD HAVE LOOKED RIGHT, AND NOTHING WAS PUT ON THE PAGE.** **A wage of four marks did not go in on the Tuesday and the number of weeks fallen due is FOURTEEN, all fourteen in the same breath.** Three of the four went home on the Tuesday afternoon, and the fourth stayed, because the man who sells lime said Saturday. **No money in and nothing out on either day; 3,596p in the bag, 2,956p short of the wage.**

### Chapter 227 — A Named Man at the End of Every Line, and a Counter Off the Fish Market
Wednesday the twenty-seventh and Thursday the twenty-eighth of January, in the shed, and in a room off the fish market. **THE CISTERN WAS CHARGED ON THE THURSDAY AND WALKED ON NONE, THE FOURTH OF FIVE. THE FAR BENCH IS NOT OCCUPIED ON EITHER DAY WITH NO FIGURE.** **THE REVERSAL, MADE AT ABOUT A QUARTER TO TEN ON THE WEDNESDAY NIGHT WITH THE LAMP LIT AND THE TWO OF THEM AND NOBODY ELSE: every line of this yard's first column since the first of May has a day at the end of it and a name at the end of it, and four lines have a day and nothing else, and those four are the four immediately above the named line of the twentieth of July, and they are still four and are still nameless and are not struck out. THE SHIPWRIGHT SAYS THAT READ AS A WHOLE THAT COLUMN IS NOT A COLUMN OF THINGS A YARD DID BUT A COLUMN OF THINGS A YARD DID WITH A MAN'S NAME AT THE END OF EVERY ONE OF THEM AND FOUR DAYS WHEN THERE WAS NO MAN'S NAME TO PUT, AND THAT IT IS A LINE OF CUSTODY, AND THAT IT IS ONLY A LINE OF CUSTODY WHERE A NAME IS AT THE END OF A LINE, AND THAT HE HAS BEEN SAYING OUT LOUD FOR FOUR VOLUMES THAT A THING A MAN CAN CHECK IS THE ONLY KIND WORTH HAVING AND HAS BEEN SAYING IT ABOUT A COLUMN WITH FOUR GAPS IN IT AND DID NOT SEE THE FOUR GAPS BECAUSE HE WAS STANDING INSIDE THE COLUMN.** The holder says that is the first thing about his own method he has not been able to see, and that neither of the two facts is about the sea. **She asks for the four to be struck out of the first column, and gives the reason herself, that a column with four days in it that have nobody's name at the end of them is a worse advertisement than a column with nothing in it at all. HE REFUSES, IN THE SAME WORDS IT IS REFUSED FOR A LINE IN ANY COLUMN: a line does not come out of a column in this book because it is an awkward thing to have been written in it, and a line comes out when it is wrong, and that is the only ground any column in this yard has ever had, and it is the same for the first column and the second and the third one the boy keeps his hand at the head of. HE WOULD STRIKE THEM OUT IF HE COULD PUT A NAME AT THE END OF THEM, AND HE CANNOT, AND THE REASON HE CANNOT IS IN THE SECOND COLUMN AND HAS BEEN SINCE THE SIXTH OF OCTOBER. I WOULD RATHER BE FOUR DAYS SHORT THAN FOUR DAYS SMOOTH.** **THE OTHER HALF: this yard has got a page nobody in this harbor can check and cannot say whether that is better or worse, so they go to a copyist of fifty-eight and ask, because she is the only person in this harbor who can put a figure on the shape of a thing. SHE IS NOT TOLD WHAT A SECOND COLUMN IS AND SHE DOES NOT ASK, and the reason is on the page: the moment a man tells a woman what a column is he is selling her a thing, and she told them to their faces that she can tell the difference between a thing a man is selling and a thing a man is showing.** The copyist refuses the question and gives the nearest thing to it she has, and says before she says it that it is about her and not about their page: a record a man can check is worth what the man is who checks it, a record nobody can check is worth what the person who keeps it is worth, there is no figure for either, and in thirty-one years she has met about nine people she would trust with a page and about four hundred she would not, and the nine are not better than the four hundred. **She says the other thing she has not looked at in thirty-one years is still coming and will be a worse thing to hear.** A penny goes out, for a sheet of paper, the first money this yard has spent in a month. **3,595p in the bag.**

### Chapter 228 — Three Nines, and a Row Counted Once, and Nine Places in a Book
Friday the twenty-ninth and Saturday the thirtieth of January, in the shed, and on the face of the second seam. **A working day, and nobody argued about anything all day, and the reason against making anything of that is that a quiet week is a week the tide was on. The far bench is entered as not occupied at any hour on either of the said two days with no figure.** **THE NINE-LINE MARK CLOSES HERE AND ONLY AS A FACT: it is a builder's mark, nine short lines joined at one point, cut in a plate by a man building the thing the plate is on, of a construction older than the calendar this coast keeps; it is NOT a claim seal and NOT a mark of anybody consenting to anything; and THIS PARTY HAS COUNTED A ROW OF NINE ONCE, IN A MONTH HE IS NOT GOING TO NAME IN A BOOK, AND IS NOT GOING TO COUNT IT TWICE, because a man who counts a thing once has said what he saw and a man who counts it twice has started a pattern and he has not got a second sight. NO OFFICE IN THIS COUNTRY CAN READ IT, and that goes in the SECOND column, and the reason is that the holder has it from two men who build stone and neither of them is an office: one who has cut stone for forty-one years and whose grandfather cut stone and who showed the holder a mark in a yard he works in and said his grandfather did not know what it was either, and his brother who does not build and says the men who come and look at that mark are men from a city with a book and that they write it down and go, and that he has never known one of them to say what it was for. The best thing he has ever heard about that mark is that no office can read it and it is not worth anything, because there are men on this coast who will tell a man a thing they cannot read for nothing at the end of a day, and he has stopped being able to tell the difference between that and an office.** **And the four nines are four different things and may not be converted into one another: nine places on a sheet of this yard's own that went on a wall in this harbor; fifty-four fastenings in about seven feet of each of the six joints at the second seam; the mark; and nine communities and nine records. THIS YARD HAS TREATED THEM AS ONE THING FOR A YEAR BECAUSE ONE OF THEM IS A NINE AND THE OTHER THREE ARE ALSO NINES AND A NUMBER IS A NUMBER, AND IT HAS TAKEN FOUR VOLUMES TO NOTICE IT. THE POINT WITH THE LIGHT IS ON THE LIST AS A PLACE AND THE LIGHT IN THE SECOND OF THE FOUR PAGES HAS NOTHING TO DO WITH IT, AND IF A MAN FOUR YEARS FROM NOW JOINS THOSE TWO HE WILL HAVE MADE IT AND NOT THIS YARD.** **THE OTHER FIVE OF THE NINE ARE NAMED AS PLACES AND NOT AS RECORDS: this harbor's own weir, the point with the light, the next town down this coast, the hard at Ashtide, and Lowfen, a chain of reed islands and floating markets two days out where there are more people living on the water than there are on it. This yard has not been to one of the nine and has not written to one and has not asked one anything.** On the Saturday morning the man of thirty-four comes back for the fourth of the four and takes it down the hill in his coat and not in his barrow, because the barrow has got chalk in it, and says he has not got a reason for that that makes any difference, and says that a page that is in a place is read by the people of that place, that it is the whole of the trade and it is not a rule, and that he has watched a page get read in a town it does not belong to and did not see anybody stop it and was one of the ones reading it. **The bench under the pump is bare for the first time since the ninth of December.** **Three marks in on the Friday; 4,075p in the bag, 3,435p short of the wage.**

### Chapter 229 — A Word That Is Not on Any of the Five, and a Corner That Came Off in the Hand
Sunday the thirty-first of January and Monday the first of February, in the shed. **THE WALL COMES DOWN BARE FOR THE THIRTY-NINTH MONDAY, THE COUNTER DID NOT RESTART, THE CLERK KEPT HIS OWN PAGE, AND NO SITTING WAS HELD. THE CISTERN WAS CHARGED ON THE MONDAY AND WALKED ON NONE, AND THAT IS THE FIFTH AND LAST OF THE FIVE THIS MONTH, AND THE NEXT ROTA DAY IS THE FOURTH OF FEBRUARY AND IS PAST THE END OF THIS BOOK. THE FAR BENCH WAS OCCUPIED ON THE MONDAY FROM ABOUT TEN UNTIL ABOUT FOUR AND NOT OCCUPIED ON THE SUNDAY AT ANY HOUR, AND THE HOLDER DID NOT GO OVER TO HIM ON EITHER DAY.** No day of work on either day, and the reason is that this yard is two days into a week without a day because the bench under the pump was bare on the Saturday and the two of them did not agree on what to put on it, and a day a yard loses for want of a decision is entered as a day the yard lost. **THE COST: on the Sunday morning they lay out the five pieces of paper this yard holds to look for one word, and the sheet and a half with no signature and no date on it came apart in the ship's hand along the fold that had gone soft, from the corner inward, about three inches, and it is not wet and is not torn and is simply not a sheet any more, and it cannot be put back. THE REASON SHE GAVE ON THE FOURTEENTH OF JANUARY FOR NOT LIFTING IT OFF THAT BENCH WAS TRUE AND WAS NOT THE REASON: THE REASON WAS THAT LIFTING IT MEANT DECIDING WHAT TO DO WITH IT, AND AS LONG AS IT WAS ON THAT BENCH UNDER A PUMP IT WAS A THING THAT HAD NOT BEEN DEALT WITH, AND THIS PARTY DEALT WITH IT.** It was worth a penny, there is no figure of a penny against it in this book, and there is nowhere on this coast a man can buy a sheet of paper with an office's name at the foot of it that nobody sent here, and it was worth four weeks of somebody looking at it and deciding not to, and she has not put a figure on that and had not slept a night since the Saturday. **THE SHAPE OF THE COST IS NAMED AND IT IS SAID WHICH SHAPE IT IS: it is not a person who has read a page and cannot unread it; it is a sheet that has been on a bench in this town for fourteen days and has gone home down that coast in the inside of a man's coat to a place two days down it, and four people are going to be asked about it this week and not one of them by this yard, and not one of the four is going to be told what two people said out loud on a Monday afternoon about a light on a point, and that is the cost and it is not anybody's fault and it is not going to be put back. **THE EARLIER FORM OF THIS — *A PAGE THAT HAS BEEN IN A SHED IN ANOTHER TOWN FOR NINE DAYS*, *FOUR PEOPLE WHO KEEP THAT PLACE*, AND *GOING TO BE TELLING THEM* — IS WITHDRAWN; IT PUT THE SHEET IN THE WRONG TOWN FOR THE WRONG NUMBER OF DAYS, ASSERTED A COMMUNICATION THIS PARTY HAS NOT MADE AND SAYS IT HAS NOT MADE, GAVE IT NO AGENT, AND COLLIDED WITH THE FOUR PEOPLE OF THE SILL.** The yard is not going to write to those four people and is not going to send anybody and is not going to put a word into the ear of the man who is going to ask them, and the reason is the reason of the fourth of October past, and the reason against the line is that this is the same ground as the four nameless lines in the second column and it has a cost already.** The read-back runs. **THE TURN: this yard has been asking people to write things down since the third of May — a man asked to keep a day, a day's cost entered against a day before the day was spent, a boat one hand short since the middle of July with four marks, one shilling and eightpence out of one part owner's own share, the man telling the ship's hand and not the holder because she writes things down — and a party which has spent four volumes insisting that a record a man can check is the only kind worth having has a page with four lines on it and a name at the head of it, saying that a page about a place is the place's, and that it is not to be carried out of the place it is about, and that a copy is not a page, and that no one of the nine is to be asked what another of the nine keeps. THAT IS A RECORD A MAN CAN CHECK AND THIS YARD HAS NEVER SEEN A BETTER ONE AND HAS NOT GOT ONE AND IS NOT GOING TO GET ONE. AND IT IS NOT A NEW THING AND IT IS SOMEBODY'S ORDINARY PRACTICE, AND THE ORDINARY OF IT IS THAT NOBODY HAD TO BE ASKED, AND A MAN OF THE FIRST LAY OF THIS TOWN SAID IN A MARKET IN THE MONTH OF DECEMBER THAT IT IS SIX CENTURIES OLD, AND THIS PARTY HAS CARRIED THAT FIGURE FOR A MONTH AND HAS NEVER HAD A LINE OF IT ON THIS YARD'S OWN PAGE. THE NAME IS ON ONE SHEET IN THIS COUNTRY THAT THIS YARD HAS EVER BEEN SHOWN AND IS ON NONE OF THE FIVE PIECES OF PAPER THIS YARD HOLDS, AND HE IS NOT GOING TO SAY THAT THAT IS A FACT ABOUT THE WORLD, BECAUSE IT IS A FACT ABOUT THIS YARD'S DRAWER. AND SIX CENTURIES IS THE AGE OF A PRACTICE AND THREE HUNDRED YEARS IS THE AGE OF AN OFFICE IN THIS COUNTRY AND THE TWO ARE NOT GOING INTO ONE SENTENCE TO MAKE A BIGGER NUMBER.** **No money in and nothing out on either day; 4,075p in the bag, 3,435p short of the wage.**

### Chapter 230 — Fifteen Weeks, a Letter Not Answered, and a Bench Under a Pump That Is Bare
Tuesday the second and Wednesday the third of February, in the shed, and on the face of the second seam. **NEITHER DAY IS A ROTA DAY AND NO CISTERN IS CHARGED IN THIS CHAPTER, and that is not a fault and is not a thing anybody has to account for: the rota runs out of days before this book does, and the next one is the fourth of February and is past the end of it. THE FAR BENCH IS NOT OCCUPIED ON EITHER DAY WITH NO FIGURE.** **THE WAGE DOES NOT GO IN, NO ENVELOPE IS MADE, AND THE NUMBER OF WEEKS FALLEN DUE STANDS AT FIFTEEN, all fifteen given in the same breath, and the reason is the reason of the twenty-ninth of December past and is not improved on.** A working day on the Wednesday, four hundred and eighty pence, a hand lead's line over the side and back four times, **and that is the last day of work this yard books in this book, and it is in the book, and it does not put one of the fifteen weeks into an envelope.** The read-back runs, and neither of them reads anything else for about two minutes, because there is nothing left in that book that is not one of the five things they have been saying for nine days. **THE QUESTION LEFT STANDING, IN THE SECOND COLUMN WITH A REASON AGAINST THE LINE: WHAT IS A COPY, AND NOT WHAT IT IS WORTH. The fourth of the four says a copy is not a page and has none of a page's standing, and that is the only sentence anybody has said to this yard in five volumes that this yard cannot argue with, because a man cannot argue with a page about what a copy is when he has got a copy in his own drawer and has never once been asked about it. There is a copy of a record of the twenty-fourth of November in that drawer and there is a copy of that copy, because a man wrote out a first column on the boards and this yard has had it in type in this harbor and in about sixty boats since the fifteenth of August, and nobody in four volumes, in any room, in any market, in any shed, has ever said out loud what a copy is. This yard has a rule from a page, a price from a copyist and no definition from anybody, and the answer to the question is either nothing or the whole of the next two things that happen to this coast, and there are twenty-five days to the first of March.** **THE COST OF THE NIGHT IS THE PRINTER AND IT IS PAID AND IT IS NOT GOING TO BE PAID TWICE: a printer in a town of about two thousand up this coast sent a letter on the twentieth of December with one question in it, being where the copy of the record is, and is not going to write again until this party writes to him, and this party has not written, and it has had a name and a question in the same drawer for six weeks and has used neither. THE HOLDER WANTS TO WRITE AND SAYS SO OUT LOUD, AND THE REASON HE HAS NOT IS THAT A PRINTER IN THAT TOWN KEEPS A LIST OF EVERYTHING THAT COMES INTO HIS SHOP THAT ANYBODY MEANS TO KEEP, AND A MAN WITH A BOOK IS A MAN THIS PARTY WOULD THEN HAVE TO BE ABLE TO LIE TO IN FOUR YEARS. IT GOES IN THE FIRST COLUMN BECAUSE A MAN IN THIS TOWN CAN GET ON THE COACH AND GO AND ASK HIM WHETHER THIS YARD HAS WRITTEN TO HIM AND BE TOLD THAT IT HAS NOT.** The shipwright enters that the holder wants to write and has wanted to for six weeks and that the reason is not the money and is not the writ, and that a man with a book is also a man who could be asked a question about a page by a person who is not the holder, and he says that is exactly right and is not going to improve on it. **THE WRIT RUNS TO MIDNIGHT ON THE FIRST OF MARCH, CARRIES FOUR NAMES AND NO FIFTH, IS NOT THE SECOND BELL LICENSE, DOES NOT LEAD TO ONE, AND RENEWS NOTHING; THIS YARD IS A PARTY OF ONE AND A HALF UNTIL THAT DAY; AND THERE ARE TWENTY-FIVE DAYS BETWEEN THE THIRD OF FEBRUARY AND THE FIRST OF MARCH, AND THAT FIGURE IS A FIGURE AND NOT A PLAN. THE SHEET AND A HALF IS NOT ON THAT BENCH AND CANNOT BE PUT BACK. THE COPY OF THE RECORD OF THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF NOVEMBER IS NINE FEET AWAY IN A DRAWER IN THAT ROOM. AND THE BATCH'S LAST LINE IS A FACT AND NOT A RESOLUTION: the bench under the pump is bare, it is the only bare thing in that shed, the shape of that bench is the shape of a sheet and a half that is not on it, and that is a fact a man can check on any day of a week, and nobody in that shed improved on it.** **Three marks in on the Wednesday; 28 marks, 6 shillings and 3 pence in the bag, being 4,555p, and 3,915p short of the wage, and that figure has gone from 1,996p on the fourteenth of January on six days and every one of the six is a day this yard worked. No sheet went up on any wall in this harbor in twenty days and none came off. The hearing room and the market wall were not used on any day of them. A working harbor of this coast is poorer on the third of February than it was on the fifteenth of January, and nothing this yard did in three weeks has made one thing better.**

## Volume 05, Batch 0004 (Chapters 231–240) — Movement 4, a person who cannot be examined, and a room that is not free

**46,740 words across ten chapters, measured with `wc -w` including headings, and 46,692 IS WITHDRAWN AS THE COUNT BEFORE THE REVIEW REPAIRS OF 27 SEPTEMBER 2026: Ch231 4,252 · Ch232 3,801 · Ch233 3,457 · Ch234 5,286 · Ch235 4,278 · Ch236 4,577 · Ch237 4,503 · Ch238 5,090 · Ch239 5,057 · Ch240 6,439. Not one chapter is inside the 2,200–3,200 guideline and all ten are past it, and the figures are on the record and are not excused; no scene was cut and no cost was dropped to bring one inside a number. The read-out device did not run in this batch and its length allowance was not used. The standing inventory ran whole in 231 and 240 and was cut to each chapter's own arithmetic in 232 to 239. The read-back ran in six chapters and 239 reverses it. Full accounting in `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0004 as written (Chapters 231–240), Volume 05*.**

**231 — *A Night at the Inside of a Bar, Said at the Head of This Dock*. Thursday the fourth and Friday the fifth of February.** The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none, the first of six. **A man of about forty of the third lay says at the head of that dock, in about nine people's hearing, that a man two days down this coast asked him what a yard in Morrow Quay had said in a shed about a light on a point, and that a man of the second lay told him on the twenty-sixth of January and did not know it had come out of a shed. He says the line back when the holder asks him to and it is the line as it stands.** The holder gives three reasons out loud for not correcting it, the last of them being that a correction is a reason and a reason does not go into a street. A working day on the Friday at the face of the second seam, twelve gross, three marks. The night's loss of the Thursday is entered in the second column with a reason against the line and no name in it. **And the ship's hand says the first thing in five volumes that has got out of this yard that the yard did not put out of it on purpose: a page has no door in it, and nobody thought about the door.** The read-back runs. Bag 5,515p, fifteen weeks.

**232 — *A Letter, and a Man on the Step Who Did Not Come In*. Saturday the sixth and Sunday the seventh.** No rota day and no charge on either. **A letter of four lines in a fair hand with no name at the foot of it comes in the ordinary post from four days up this coast**, saying that a sheet of this yard's own days is in three houses in that place, was read out in a kitchen to about nine people, and that a place that has a yard's days in its houses ought to be asked which of them it wants and not told. **A man of about fifty comes up the hill on the Sunday and does not come in**, standing four feet off the door, and says four men in a place two days down this coast have all read a page and none of them is going to put it down, and that the youngest says it is the first thing anybody has shown him that says a place is allowed to decide things about itself and cannot say it out loud there. No day of work on either. **The two of them disagree and the disagreement is entered in one sentence, and this yard is not writing to either of them.** Bag 5,515p.

**233 — *Ten Words in a Market, and a Slate That Came Down Bare*. Monday the eighth and Tuesday the ninth.** The fortieth wipe, bare, no sitting. The cistern is charged on the Monday and walked on none, the second of six. **The Second Bell form of the fourth of December is answered in a market, in about forty people's hearing, in ten words by the ship's hand, standing at the head of that dock and not on the boards: *It is here. It was not sent on the day.* The holder of the writ did not go down the hill and did not speak in that street, and this yard is not on the boards.** One line in that half sheet was not said and is not going to be said in a market. **The price is named at the top of that bench in the same afternoon, after the answer and not before it: about forty people now know where this yard's copy of a record is, and a yard that has spent five volumes saying a thing nobody can check is worth nothing has told a market where the thing it cannot check is.** The wage does not go in and the weeks fallen due stand at sixteen, all sixteen in the same breath. Bag 5,515p.

**234 — *A Slate That Is Wiped on Monday, and a Price Named Before the Yes*. Wednesday the tenth and Thursday the eleventh.** **The hearing room over the tide-gang store is used again and the tide gang loses a second morning to its floor**, named on the mole's steps and not in the room. **The reeve names the price out loud in front of about eleven people before the yes**, and says out loud why he may be in a room this month when he was not one last month: his ledger is spent, his repair account has been empty since the middle of February, and he has about three things left, and this is the biggest, and it is bigger than the tenth of December because that one was a thing about a man and a notice and this one is a thing about what a piece of paper is. He writes five lines in chalk on a slate that comes down at eight on Monday, the clerk copies them into the file, and the holder reads them back.** **And he says that a plate in eleven feet is not reached by a better letter out of that shed, and that the only mechanism anybody has named for reaching it, including him, is a change of politics in an office.** The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none, the third of six. The read-back runs. Bag 5,515p.

**235 — *The Half She Has Not Looked At in Thirty-One Years, and a Penny*. Friday the twelfth and Saturday the thirteenth.** No rota day. A working day at the face of the second seam, and the man of the second lay stayed for the whole of it for the first time since the eighteenth of January and gave no reason. **At a counter in a room off the fish market on the Saturday, Pernel Saye gives the other half of her trade, which she has not looked at in thirty-one years, and says beforehand that it is about her and not about a page and that she is telling them because they asked the other question first.** She has a copy in her own back room that she made nine years ago for a man who did not come back, has never once looked at it, does not know what is on it, and it is the only thing she has ever made that she cannot account for to anybody. **She refused this yard's three parts — not to see it, not to be told where it is, not to be offered it — and the reason is on the page and is not a favour, and she said she read four lines of that yard's book at half past seven that morning and put it back.** A sheet of paper is bought for one penny and the penny is entered: the third money this yard has spent since the eighteenth of December, being a penny on the eighteenth of December past, a penny on the twenty-eighth of January instant and this penny. Bag 5,994p, less a penny.

**236 — *The Thirty-Eighth Line, and a Man Who Counted It in July*. Sunday the fourteenth and Monday the fifteenth.** The forty-first wipe, bare. The cistern is charged on the Monday and walked on none, the fourth of six. **The batch's discovery: the printed broadside is thirty-eight lines, thirty-seven of them days from the sixteenth of June to the twenty-second of July, and the thirty-eighth at the foot says there is a copy in about sixty boats on this coast, that a man who has one and finds it changed is to say so to the yard that printed it, and that any man may go in there and read it and see where the other ones are. The party discovers that the line was never a defect, that the thing it was afraid of and the thing it was proud of are the same line, and that a man who built it in July said so in a room and was right.** Thirty-three of the thirty-seven end in a man's name and four end in nothing, and those four are in about sixty boats and cannot be unprinted. **A compositor of forty-one says nobody asked him for the thirty-eighth line and says what the holder said to him on the fourth of August, and says he has set it four times since and has one line in twenty-two years with no name on it that is this yard's.** A working day on the Monday, thirteen gross, three marks. Bag 6,474p.

**237 — *Two Shillings and Tenpence in Another Man's Book*. Tuesday the sixteenth and Wednesday the seventeenth.** No rota day. **The wage does not go in and the weeks fallen due stand at seventeen, all seventeen in the same breath. A cost of a shape of its own is paid: a compositor comes up the hill unasked with his own book and a figure of two shillings and tenpence for the fourth of January, twenty-two hours in two days entered as one day, and it goes in this yard's FIRST COLUMN in his own figure out of his own book and not out of this yard's money, and the reason against the line is that a day this yard's money did not lose is not a figure this yard's money lost.** The holder will not say sorry to him and says why on the page, and he says the figure is not nothing and asks that the book be left where it is and stand open. **The shape of sentence is said once in this batch, by the ship's hand, about that day and that funeral, and nobody remarks on it.** The night of the Wednesday is lost and is entered in the second column with a reason against the line and no name in it. The read-back runs. Bag 6,474p.

**238 — *A List of Names Is a List of Who Can Be Asked What*. Thursday the eighteenth and Friday the nineteenth.** The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none, the fifth of six, and the sixth is entered as one left. A working day at the face of the second seam. **THE TURN. At about six on the Friday evening, unasked, in about eleven people's hearing, a sailmaker of sixty of the second lay with a boy of seventeen in one of the sixty boats says that the printed sheet is not a list of days, it is a list of names, and that a list of names is a list of who can be asked what.** He says everything above the last line is a receipt and the last line is an order and the order is the one anybody uses; that the four lines in the middle of July are the only part of that sheet that says a man is not to be asked; that he found them in October and has told nobody for four months; and that he has a place he can now go and ask about his own boy and has not used it and is not going to. **The ship's hand says the party is not going to unprint the sheet, is not going to improve it, and is not going to add a thirty-ninth line saying that no man is to be asked anything, because a thirty-ninth line would be an order as well and there is nobody in this country to send it to — and that the thirty-eighth line was not the problem, and the thirty-three names were, and they were the evidence, and this yard has been calling that page evidence since the fifteenth of August and it is not evidence and never was.** The read-back runs. Bag 6,954p.

**239 — *Four Places, Four Readings, and a False Thing Said About a Man in a Kitchen*. Saturday the twentieth and Sunday the twenty-first.** No rota day. **A reader of fifty-five comes up the mole unasked at a quarter to eight, says he has read the sheet out eleven times and will not read it twice for the same penny, and gives four accounts of what four men said to him about it: a map, a directory, a thing to be said out loud at a funeral, and a set of days that ought to be asked about — and says every one of the four is right and that he is not going to say the same thing four times so that it comes out even.** **And he gives the fifth thing, which is the price: he has been called a man of this yard in about forty people's hearing in a room four days' walk from here, he has let it stand, he has a daughter of nineteen who goes to the kitchen where the sheet was read, and the reason he let it stand is that the true sentence about him is worse than the false one.** **At ten past two the ship's hand puts her right hand into a pump handle with a split in it and comes out of it with the two middle fingers opened across the knuckles; a bonesetter puts four stitches in it at about six and charges two shillings, and the Sunday has no board written on it.** **The read-back runs and IS REVERSED — the holder reads from the top of the bench and the ship's hand reads back after him — and the chapter says it is the first time in five volumes the day's entry has been read that way, and gives the reason: the lamp was turned round so the light was not on the page, and there was nothing else in that day to read.** Bag 7,410p, three marks less the bonesetter.

**240 — *The Forty-Second Wipe, Eighteen Weeks, and a Line in a Lane*. Monday the twenty-second and Tuesday the twenty-third.** The forty-second wipe, bare, and the five lines chalked on that wall on the eleventh came down with it and were not copied into the file by anybody. **The cistern is charged on the Monday and walked on none, the sixth and last, and that is the twenty-third of the charges walked on none since the third of August past, and the next rota day is a Monday and this party entered nothing about it.** The far bench is occupied on the Monday and the holder does not go over. **No board on the Monday, because the woman has four stitches in her right hand and a yard of two does not work a Monday when one of the two cannot hold a line, and that is entered as a day the yard lost and not as a day the yard did not know about, and there is no figure against it because there is no figure for a day.** The holder goes down to the compositor's room on his own account and is told one sheet is a penny and fourpence and that the thirty-eighth line is the one that costs, and hears that the sheet has now been set five times, the fifth for a place he had never heard of, and that he has been looking for a line in his own book that he cannot put a figure against. **The wage does not go in and the weeks fallen due stand at EIGHTEEN, all eighteen in the same breath. The question is left standing that this batch does not answer: what a yard owes for a line it printed in good faith and cannot get back — answered with a second-column fact and a reason against the line, and the reason is the reason of the fourth of October and is not going to be given in that shed or in a market or in a room or in four years.** The read-back runs. **The batch's last line is a fact and not a resolution: the bench under that pump is bare for twenty-four nights, since a sheet and a half came apart in the ship's hand on that mole on the Sunday of the thirty-first of January, and it is the only bare thing in that shed, and the shape of that bench is the shape of a sheet and a half that came up this hill on foot on the ninth of December and came apart in a hand on the thirty-first and cannot be put back and cannot be bought. There are five days between that Tuesday and the first of March, and the writ of the first of May runs to midnight on the first of March, and this yard is a party of one and a half until that day.** Nobody in that shed improved on it. Bag 7,890p, short of the wage by 7,250p.

## Volume 05, Batch 0005 (Chapters 241–250) — Movement 5, a living archive and a paper that takes the charter. **THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX AND THE VOLUME'S LAST TEN CHAPTERS.**

**REVIEWED AND REPAIRED, 27 SEPTEMBER 2026. A reviewer pass over the ten chapters, the four state files, `outline/volume-05.md`, the queued `workspace/volume-05/close/PROMPT.md` and the Volume 04 audit returned **FIX** with seven required changes, four serious craft and moderate findings and three minor, and ALL FOURTEEN ARE APPLIED. **NO CHAPTER WAS RESTARTED, NO SCENE WAS CUT, NO COST WAS DROPPED, NO DAY MOVED, NO LOCK WAS SOFTENED AND NO PLANNED PLOT WAS ALTERED**, and the full disposition is `state/continuity.md` § 17, which is the authority for them, with the thread-level consequences in `state/open-threads.md` § *Volume 05, Batch 0005 — review repairs*. Three chapters were touched: **Chapter 244's dateline, which named two places four hundred miles apart that the chapter is not in, now reads *in the shed, at the foot of that mole, and on the road out of it*; Chapter 247's sentence that said the man of about thirty-four *was in the room* now says *was not in the room*, on the point that is the batch's resolution; and Chapter 249's closing inventory now gives the whole of what went out in the twenty days as three marks, one shilling and one penny, being 493p, which is what Chapter 250 and the money table already said.** Also corrected in the state files: the far-bench list, which contradicted all ten chapters and gave nine days occupied and nine not where the chapters give six and fourteen; the figure *nine* for the holder's absence, which is fourteen; the drawer, which is six pieces of paper and holds the printer's letter; the read-back's chapter allocation; Batch 0002's figure in `state/current.md`; the sixth-of-March row of the money table; `state/continuity.md` § 11's claim that the reason for Chapter 250's gross of fourteen is on the page and is the sea; and § 16's own log, which was short two items. The count of the two objects of nobody's was checked against the page and is right at 277.**

**41,196 words across ten chapters, measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline: Ch241 4,364 · Ch242 3,893 · Ch243 3,293 · Ch244 3,979 · Ch245 4,476 · Ch246 3,591 · Ch247 4,688 · Ch248 3,775 · Ch249 3,885 · Ch250 5,252. THE FIGURES 41,194, 41,191, 5,546, 4,361, 3,980, 4,687 AND 3,883 ARE WITHDRAWN AND ARE NAMED AS WITHDRAWN HERE RATHER THAN SILENTLY REPLACED; 41,191 and 4,361 are the reserved-sentence repair in Chapter 241 at `state/continuity.md` § 16 items 2 and 4, and the rest are the reviewer pass of 27 September 2026 at `state/continuity.md` § 17 items 1 to 3 — one word out of Chapter 244's dateline, one word into Chapter 247, two words into Chapter 249 — and the volume total is 208,243. THIS IS 5,544 WORDS UNDER BATCH 0004'S 46,740 AND ONLY CHAPTER 243 IS INSIDE THE GUIDELINE. THE FIGURE THAT DECIDED THE LENGTH WAS NOT THE PROSE AND WAS THE HOUSE'S OWN APPARATUS — a first column of between thirteen hundred and twenty-two hundred words, a second column of two to four hundred, and a standing inventory of one thousand two hundred and fifty-one words whole, which is a floor of about two thousand two hundred before a writer says anything. NO SCENE WAS CUT TO SHORTEN ANY CHAPTER AND NO COST WAS DROPPED TO BRING ONE INSIDE A NUMBER.** Wednesday the twenty-fourth of February to Monday the fifteenth of March, twenty consecutive days, two days to a chapter. **The read-out device did not run and its allowance was not claimed. The standing inventory ran whole in 241 and 250 and was cut to each chapter's own arithmetic in 242 to 249. The read-back ran in five chapters and six instances — **241 once, 243 twice, 244, 247 and 250 once each**, the ship's hand reading first in every one of them; the earlier allocation, which gave 241 twice, is withdrawn, and the method is at `state/continuity.md` § 1 and § 17 item 8. The shape of sentence was said once, in 249. THE HOLDER OF THE WRIT IS UP THAT COAST ROAD FOR FOURTEEN OF THE TWENTY DAYS, being every day from the second to the fifteenth of March, and the figure *nine* that three files gave is withdrawn. THE FAR BENCH WAS OCCUPIED ON SIX OF THE TWENTY DAYS, being the twenty-sixth of February and the fourth, sixth, eighth, twelfth and fourteenth of March. THE DRAWER ON THOSE BENCHES HOLDS SIX PIECES OF PAPER AT THE CLOSE OF CHAPTER 250 AND THE PRINTER'S LETTER IS IN IT. THE COUNT OF THE TWO OBJECTS OF NOBODY'S IS 277 AND IS RIGHT.**

**241 — *What the Holder of the Writ Is on the First of March*. Wednesday the twenty-fourth and Thursday the twenty-fifth of February.** The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none, the first of six. No work on the Wednesday; a working day on the Thursday. **The chapter's work is a finding and the finding is a fact and not a cliffhanger: at the counter of this harbor's own authority a clerk says the writ of the first of May is a municipal instrument, inside the harbor line, bounded by the outer marks, four names and no fifth, not the Second Bell license, does not lead to one, renews nothing, and runs to midnight on the first of March, and that the office which issued it is not the office that can renew it, so at midnight that paper stops being a thing the holder holds and nothing takes its place, and nobody is charged with anything, no court sits, nobody is fined, and no clerk comes up that hill. THE CHARTER IS NAMED AND IT IS A DIFFERENT THING AND A CLERK'S MOUTH KEEPS THE TWO APART ON THE PAGE.** The read-back runs twice, and the ship's hand refuses the holder's sentence in the second column and puts her own there.

**242 — *Two Things That Came Up the Coast, and the Price of Each Before the Day*. Friday the twenty-sixth and Saturday the twenty-seventh.** No rota day. A working day on the Friday. **A man of about forty-five comes up the coast road with a cart and a book of ground about water about nine miles from his own door, in water no lead and no boat of that harbor has been in, kept by four people alive, and it does not come out of the place it is about, because a page about a place is the place's and if it leaves it stops being the place's and starts being evidence. IN THE SAME WEEK A MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR COMES UP THE HILL IN THE RAIN WITH A LEAD ABOUT THE HOLDER'S OWN BROTHER — a man of about fifty, four days down that coast, a boat of about thirty tons, two days, a month two years back — AND HE HAD ASKED THAT MAN NOTHING. THE TWO ARRIVE INSIDE TWO DAYS AND THE CHAPTER DOES NOT RESOLVE EITHER, AND THE PRICE OF THE LEAD IS NAMED BEFORE THE DAY: four days there and four days back, two men off the water, about twenty-four marks, and it is not paid and is not going to be paid.** No confession, no fragment, no report talk, no second story, and the holder does not go over to the far bench.

**243 — *Midnight on the First of March, and Who Is Going to Be Told Something*. Sunday the twenty-eighth of February and Monday the first of March.** The forty-third wipe, bare, no sitting. The cistern is charged on the Monday and walked on none, the second of six. **THE WRIT RUNS TO MIDNIGHT ON THIS DAY AND AT TEN MINUTES PAST MIDNIGHT THE HOLDER ENTERS IN THE FIRST COLUMN IN HIS OWN LEFT HAND THAT IT CEASED TO BE A THING THAT PARTY HOLDS, AND THE SHIP'S HAND IS NOT USED FOR THAT LINE BECAUSE A PAGE A MAN CANNOT DATE HIMSELF IS A PAGE A MAN CANNOT CHECK. NO COURT SAT, NO CLERK CAME UP THAT HILL, NOBODY WAS CHARGED WITH ANYTHING, NOBODY WAS FINED, NOBODY WAS REFUSED ANYTHING, NOBODY WAS EXAMINED. A PARTY WHICH IS NOT DOING A THING ON A DAY IS NOT FINED, BECAUSE A COURT DOES NOT FINE A POOR MAN, AND THE MATTER SIMPLY GOES ON, AND WHAT IS GONE IS AN INSTRUMENT AND NOT A MAN.** And on that day the four communities and the other places are being told something by somebody, and that somebody is not this yard. A working day on the Monday. The read-back runs.

**244 — *The Living Archive and the Lead, and the Day He Did Not Take the Lead*. Tuesday the second and Wednesday the third of March. THE VOLUME'S CLIMAX.** No rota day. **The wage does not go in and the number of weeks fallen due is NINETEEN, all nineteen in the same breath, and the reason is the reason of the twenty-ninth of December and is not improved on. THE COST OF THE GOING WAS NAMED IN THE FIRST COLUMN ON THE MONDAY NIGHT AT ABOUT ELEVEN O'CLOCK, BEFORE THE DAY AND NOT AFTER IT, AND AT ABOUT TEN ON THE TUESDAY MORNING THE HOLDER GETS ON THE CART AND GOES UP THAT COAST ROAD WITH THE MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FIVE. ON THE WEDNESDAY AFTERNOON THE MAN WHO BROUGHT THE LEAD SAYS HE IS GOING FOUR DAYS DOWN THAT COAST ON THE FRIDAY IN HIS OWN CART ON HIS OWN ACCOUNT AND IS NOT WAITING AND HAS NOT BEEN ASKED TO WAIT, AND NOBODY IN THAT SHED SAYS ONE WORD, BECAUSE THE PRICE WAS NAMED AND NOT PAID. THE LEAD DOES NOT COME BACK. NOBODY IN THAT SHED IMPROVES ON THAT.** The third of the three costs of a thing on the water is paid on the page: it takes a crew, a crew is people, and a party of two has no crew in it. The read-back runs.

**245 — *Thirty-One, and a Form With a Name on It*. Thursday the fourth and Friday the fifth of March.** The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none, the third of six. **THIRTY-ONE IS SAID OUT LOAD FOR THE FOURTH TIME IN SEVEN MONTHS AT ABOUT SEVEN IN THE MORNING AT THE HEAD OF THAT DOCK IN ABOUT ELEVEN PEOPLE'S HEARING, WITH THE THREE COUNTS AT TWENTY-TWO AND THIRTEEN AND ELEVEN IN ONE BREATH, THE REFUSAL OF THE THIRTY-FIRST OF MARCH UNREVERSED, AND THE YARD SAYING IN THE SAME BREATH THAT IT WILL NOT SAY IT A FIFTH TIME. A BOAT LIES OFF THE NORTH SIDE AND A SEALED ENVELOPE COMES UP THAT MOLE IN A HAND WITH A NAME ON THE OUTSIDE OF IT AND NOBODY COMES UP THE HILL WITH IT, AND THE NAME IS ENTERED IN THAT FIRST COLUMN IN FULL AND HE IS IN NO ROOM. THE FORM IS NOT A WRIT, NOT A LICENCE, NOT A FINDING, NOTHING IN IT IS A SUSPENSION AND NOTHING IN IT IS A PENALTY, A PARTY WHICH DOES NOT PRODUCE THE THING ON THE DAY IS NOT FINED BECAUSE A COURT DOES NOT FINE A POOR MAN, A PARTY PRODUCING THE CHARTER MAY ENTER IN WRITING ONCE ANY PARTICULAR IT WISHES RECORDED, THE PARTY MAY NOT BE REQUIRED TO PRODUCE ANY BOOK OF ITS OWN IN ANY COLUMN, AND NOTHING IN IT REQUIRES A PARTY TO SAY WHO ALTERED ANYTHING OR AT WHOSE INSTANCE OR WITH WHAT INTENT. THE CHARTER CAME OUT OF THAT DRAWER AT ABOUT TWO IN THE AFTERNOON AND WENT DOWN THAT MOLE IN A HAND, AND THE HAND IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT THIS BOOK SAYS ABOUT WHO TOOK IT.** A clerk's mouth keeps the two instruments apart a second time and says the harbor authority is not the office that can give that charter back and never was. A working day on the Friday.

**246 — *One Page, One Drawer, One Town, and One Hand*. Saturday the sixth and Sunday the seventh.** No rota day. **THE CHAIN OF CUSTODY IS MADE HERE AND IT IS NEW AND IT IS NOT AGED AND IT IS NOT EXPLAINED BY ANYBODY WHO COULD NOT HAVE INVENTED IT. A PAGE ABOUT THAT HARBOR IS MADE IN THAT HARBOR IN THE SHIP'S OWN HAND AND PUT IN THE DRAWER ON THOSE BENCHES AT ABOUT TWO IN THE AFTERNOON, AND IT SAYS ON ITS FACE THAT IT IS NOT A DOCUMENT AND NOT A RECORD AND NOT EVIDENCE AND NOT A RETURN AND NOT A COPY OF ANYTHING. FOUR SHEETS GO INTO THE ORDINARY POST AT ABOUT HALF PAST FOUR, ADDRESSED TO FOUR PLACES AND NOT ONE NAME, AT FOURPENCE, AND THE PRICE OF A GOING WAS NAMED BEFORE THE GOING AND THIS YARD HAS SENT NOBODY TO ANY OF THEM. THE CHAPTER SAYS WHOSE HAND PUT THE PAGE IN AND WHAT THE OTHER FOUR CAN DO ABOUT IT, AND IT IS ONE PAGE IN ONE DRAWER IN ONE TOWN AND NOT FIVE.** No institution, no new body, no name. A working day on the Sunday.

**247 — *Five Hands, and Why the Thing Has No Name on It*. Monday the eighth and Tuesday the ninth of March.** The forty-fourth wipe, bare. The cistern is charged on the Monday and walked on none, the fourth of six. **The wage does not go in and the number of weeks fallen due is TWENTY, all twenty in the same breath.** A copyist of fifty-eight is asked the question of the name at her counter and answers because she was asked the other question first, and says that a name is a thing an office can be asked for and there is no office to ask, and that that is the whole reason and there is not a second reason behind it. **SHE SAYS THE PART ABOUT THE LIMIT, BECAUSE THE LIMIT IS FOUR PEOPLE'S AND NOT THAT YARD'S: a wall with no door in it is the only kind four people of that coast can stand behind, because there is no office on that coast that can put a door in it. AND SHE SAYS THE PRICE: a page in a drawer in five towns is a page nobody can check, and a record a man can check is worth what the man who checks it is worth, and there is no figure for either and there never has been.** And five hands write five lines on one sheet with nothing at the top of it, and the holder of the writ's hand is not on it.

**248 — *The Same Question, the Second Time, and Still Not Answered*. Wednesday the tenth and Thursday the eleventh of March.** A working day on the Wednesday. The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none, the fifth of six. **A man of about sixty of the first lay, forty-one years on that water, asks in a market in about forty people what an office that has kept records for three hundred years was guarding against at the point at which it decided what it was allowed to lose, and says he asked it in December and is not going to wait for a man up a coast road. THE YARD'S ANSWER THE SECOND TIME IS THREE FIGURES AND A REASON AGAINST THE LINE AND NO NAME IN IT, GIVEN IN THAT ORDER ON PURPOSE BECAUSE A FIGURE THAT IS RIGHT AND IN THE WRONG PLACE IS A LIE: NINE COMMUNITIES AND THE RECORDS ONE IN EACH, SIX CENTURIES OF THAT PRACTICE, THREE HUNDRED YEARS OF THE OFFICE THAT ANSWERS IT. SHE SAYS OUT LOAD BEFORE SHE GIVES THEM THAT NONE OF THEM IS AN ANSWER AND THAT SHE IS NOT GOING TO CALL A FACT AN ANSWER, AND THE REASON IS A PERSON OF NO OFFICE AND NOT AN OFFICE.** And two more things in the same street, neither of them a victory: the Crown could not make this yard say who altered anything and was not asked to, and this yard cannot make any one of the four communities trust it and has not tried to.

**249 — *Five Drawers, Five Hands, and a Thing on the Water That Has to Be Looked At*. Friday the twelfth and Saturday the thirteenth of March.** No rota day. A working day on the Friday. **FOUR LINES FROM FOUR TOWNS, EACH A DIFFERENT THING DONE TO A PAGE IN A DRAWER, AND ALL FOUR ARE RIGHT — a kitchen on a Sunday to about nine people and never again; one person reading it and telling nobody; a page not opened since it came, with the keeper writing that down; a page copied out once and hung in a room people come into — AND THIS YARD ASKED NONE OF THEM FOR AN ANSWER AND CANNOT GO AND LOOK IN ANY OF THE FOUR DRAWERS.** And at about a quarter past two a boat of about forty tons goes out with a mark on a line over her side and about eleven people on that mole see her go. **THE BELLWRIGHT STAGE IS TAKEN HERE AND ALL THREE OF ITS COSTS ARE PAID ON THE PAGE: maintenance, because a thing on the water has to be looked at and no figure in that country can be given for how many times; public exposure, because a thing on the water is a thing on a wall that moves; and dependence on a trained crew, because two men were paid out of that bag for a day's work the two of them would do between them, and the season of the mark is nine marks and is not going to be paid and nobody is going to be asked for it.** The case of five bells is not raised and the limit four people of that coast wrote and signed is quoted in that yard's own first column and stands. **The whole of what went out of that yard in the twenty days ended on the said Saturday is three marks, one shilling and one penny, being four hundred and ninety-three pence, and that is the figure the chapter now gives.**

**250 — *Five Drawers, a Wall Where There Has Never Been One, a Boat, a Name Beside a Hand, and a Second Written Down*. Sunday the fourteenth and Monday the fifteenth of March. THE VOLUME'S LAST CHAPTER.** A working day on the Sunday, and at a quarter past two a person goes out in a boat of about forty tons to look at a mark fouled with about four inches of green that is making a small noise in a flat calm a man on a mole could not have heard, and nobody struck it. **The forty-fifth wipe, bare. The cistern is charged on the Monday and walked on none, the sixth and last of six. THE WAGE DOES NOT GO IN AND THE NUMBER OF WEEKS FALLEN DUE STANDS AT TWENTY, all twenty in the same breath. THE VOLUME'S FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE IS PAID IN FULL: five drawers in five towns each with a page in it and no two of them with the same page; a page going up on a market wall in a town four days up this coast where there has never been one, carrying the place of every copy of itself; a person going out in a boat; the boy not on the stone and the third column with a name in it that is not the boy's and is not the holder's; and the delay, a hand's width of a second, with nobody having said why and it written down anyway with a date against it, which is not a measurement and not an occasion. AND THE RESERVED SENTENCE IS SAID OUT LOUD IN A MARKET IN ABOUT FORTY PEOPLE'S HEARING BY A HERRING SKIPPER OF FIFTY-ONE, AND IT IS THE LAST TRUE THING ANYBODY SAYS IN THIS VOLUME: A PERSON WHO IS NOT EXAMINED IS NOT A PERSON WHO HAS BEEN REFUSED.** And the volume's closing position, in the last words of the book: a working harbor poorer on the fifteenth of March than it was on the twenty-third of February, and a yard that has learned one new trade and cannot yet pay a man to do it with.
