THEY ARE LEFT AS THEY ARE, BECAUSE A MISSING FINAL NEWLINE IS NOT A PROSE FAULT AND NO REPAIR IN THIS AUDIT TOUCHES ONE.**

### The one repair, and where it landed

**AT `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0231.md` LINES 21, 87 AND 103, THE LAST CARRYING IT TWICE, *THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF JANUARY* BECOMES *THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF JANUARY*, ONE WORD IN FOUR PLACES, LOCK L1, AND THE CHAPTER'S WORDCOUNT IS UNCHANGED AT 4,252, SO NO FIGURE IN THE TABLE ABOVE MOVED.**

**THE DATE IS WRONG AND THE WEEKDAY IS RIGHT, ON FOUR INDEPENDENT GROUNDS: Chapter 226 stages the reading on the Monday the twenty-fifth; Chapter 226's own day line reads *Monday the twenty-fifth and Tuesday the twenty-sixth of January* and twenty-nine chapters' worth of the inherited wage list makes the twenty-sixth a Tuesday; the same second-column sentence says the line went into a street *inside an hour*; and the first column of the same chapter has the holder refusing to say what two people said in a shed to a man of the third lay *on a Monday afternoon*.** The fifth mention of the twenty-sixth on that chapter, in the inherited wage list at line 107, is correct and was not touched. **After the repair, all 192 weekday-and-date pairs on the fifty pages are correct, and the two that disagree with the real 2026 almanac are the two inherited wage weeks of August and September, which agree with the chapters that staged them and which lock L1a item 2 expressly permits.**

### The money, re-derived and closed twice

**THE WHOLE OF VOLUME 05 CLOSES IN ONE LINE: 717 + (23 × 480) − 520 = 11,237. THE LAST TWENTY DAYS CLOSE IN THEIR OWN LINE: 7,890 + (8 × 480) − 4 − 489 = 11,237.** The volume opens on a bag of four marks and six shillings and five pence, being seven hundred and seventeen pence, and closes on seventy marks, three shillings and one penny. **The whole of what came in in a hundred days is twenty-three days at the face of the second seam at four hundred and eighty pence, on hirers' own accounts, with nobody in the water, and no two of the twenty-three were worked by the same two people. The whole of what went out is five hundred and twenty pence in six transactions at four counters.** Every one of the twenty-eight readings of the bag in the fifty chapters is the correct rendering of the marks, shillings and pence beside it.

### The eleven things a later phase must not re-derive wrongly

1. **THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN VOLUME 05 AND THE COUNT IS ZERO.** The body is charged and a day lost to it carries no figure. The four nameless lines above the named line of the twentieth of July past are still four and are in about sixty boats.
2. **THERE WERE FOUR RETURNS AND NO FIFTH, AND NO FINDING OF WORKED GLASS WAS MADE AND NOTHING WAS RAISED ON ANY OF THE HUNDRED DAYS.**
3. **THE PLATE NOTE IS NOT PAID. IT IS PART. IT IS CARRIED OPEN, FOR THE THIRD VOLUME IN A ROW.** The document that would have closed it is spent, and the volume said out loud in a character's mouth that the only mechanism named for reaching that plate is a change of politics in an office and not a better letter.
4. **THE THIRD COLUMN, THE BOY AND THE NAME THAT WENT IN.** The column was not filled on any day of the hundred and nobody was appointed and nobody was hired to read it back, and on the fifteenth of March a name went in beside the boy's hand and not over it and it is neither the boy's nor the holder's. **Lock L21's three prohibitions are observed and the outline's final chapter image is paid, and the resolution was done in the prose and not in the author's head.**
5. **THE THREE FIGURES THAT MUST NOT BE SWAPPED ARE ON THE PAGE OF CHAPTER 248 IN THAT ORDER:** nine communities and the records one in each, six centuries of that practice, and three hundred years of the office that answers it. **The office's three hundred years was not handed to the communities.**
6. **THE RESERVED SENTENCE IS SPENT ONCE, IN CHAPTER 250, IN A MARKET, IN ABOUT FORTY PEOPLE'S HEARING, AND IT IS THE LAST TRUE THING ANYBODY SAYS IN THE VOLUME.** It is also on one page of Chapters 201 to 249, `chapter-0215.md`, twice, as the printed text of a half sheet of a court, and it was not said out loud in that harbor on that day by anybody, which that chapter says on its own face.
7. **THE COMPACT'S NAME IS ON EXACTLY ONE PAGE OF THE FIFTY — `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0225.md` line 27 — and it is in no mouth on any other page and on no wall.** It is called the name at the head of the fourth of the four everywhere it is needed. **The compact is not confirmed, the nine communities did not make one body, and the agreement did not hold.**
8. **THE CHAIN OF CUSTODY IS NEW, IS A PRACTICE, AND MAY NOT BE DATED EARLIER THAN THE SIXTH OF MARCH 2027.** Five pages in five drawers in five towns in five hands, one page to a drawer, no two of them the same page, with an agreement about who may read a page that has no name.
9. **THE DELAYED NOTE STANDS AT ELEVEN AND TWO, NEITHER COUNT WAS ADVANCED, NOTHING WAS MEASURED, AND THE FIGURE WAS WRITTEN DOWN ONCE WITH A DATE AGAINST IT — WHICH IS NOT A MEASUREMENT AND NOT AN OCCASION.**
10. **THE FAR-BENCH FIGURE FOR THE WHOLE FIFTY CHAPTERS IS THIRTEEN DAYS, COUNTED FROM THE PROSE, in five batches of 3 / 1 / 1 / 2 / 6.** He was not asked for anything on any of the hundred days and the holder did not go over to him on any of them.
11. **THE HOUSE'S LENGTH RULE IS MEASURING THE HOUSE'S OWN APPARATUS.** The apparatus is 85,299 words across the fifty chapters, which is **41.0%** of the volume, and it runs from 878 words a chapter to 2,650 with a median of 1,667. **The 2,200-word lower bound sits inside the apparatus of a median chapter of this volume. The drift the house has been measuring is a measurement failure and not a prose failure.** The Batch 0005 section's own figures — a first column of between thirteen hundred and twenty-two hundred, a second of two to four hundred, and a standing inventory of 1,251 words whole — **do not measure and are corrected with the method beside them: 954 to 1,941, 302 to 667, and the closing block of Chapter 250 at 1,622.**

### The duplication audit, run over all fifty chapters

**THREE PASSES PLUS A WITHIN-FILE PASS, WITH THE SPLITTER STRIPPING EMPHASIS MARKS AND NORMALISING WHITESPACE, AND THE TOTALS GIVEN WITH THE METHOD BECAUSE A TOTAL WITHOUT ITS SPLITTER IS NOT RE-DERIVABLE.** One byte-identical duplicate paragraph of twelve words or more across the fifty chapters, **and it is the working day's board, byte-identical in Chapters 217, 228, 230 and 231, RULED AT `reviews/volume-04/volume-04-close.md` § 21b AND NOT REPAIRED**, and the figures are the same in all four instances because a twelve-gross day at the mole pays the yard the same three marks. Fifteen forty-word opening matches, all standing entries or the house's own column framing. One proper-containment pair, a man quoting his own sentence. Seventy exact sentences of eight words or more appearing in more than one chapter, every one a standing inventory line, a standing column, a form's printed text or the house's own device, distributed 20 / 9 / 7 / 4 / 2 / 8 / 7 / 5 / 6 / 1 / 1 by number of chapters. **Zero within-file repeated sentences of eight words or more.** One verbatim quotation of a signed document, named as a quotation and not a fault.

### The volume's shape, stated on its own face

**FIVE MOVEMENTS, FIFTY CHAPTERS, ONE HUNDRED CONSECUTIVE DAYS FROM SUNDAY THE SIXTH OF DECEMBER 2026 TO MONDAY THE FIFTEENTH OF MARCH 2027, TWO DAYS TO A CHAPTER, NO DAY MISSING AND NO DAY IN TWO CHAPTERS, NO CHAPTER OF THE VOLUME STATES A YEAR, AND NO DIVE AND NO PERSON OF THIS YARD IN ANY WATER ON ANY OF THE HUNDRED DAYS. THE CENTRAL PRESSURE RESOLVED IN A DRAWER AND NOT IN A SPEECH; THE MIDPOINT PAID IN A SHED AND BY TWO PEOPLE; THE CLIMAX PAID IN ALL FOUR OF ITS ELEMENTS; THE RESOLUTION PAID IN ALL THIRTEEN ITEMS IN THE OUTLINE'S ORDER; THE NEW-VOLUME QUESTION ASKED A SECOND TIME AND NOT ANSWERED; THE FINAL CHAPTER IMAGE PAID IN ALL FIVE OF ITS ELEMENTS IN CHAPTER 250; AND THE VOLUME'S OWN CLOSING POSITION PAID IN THE LAST WORDS OF THE BOOK: a working harbor poorer on the fifteenth of March than it was on the twenty-third of February, and a yard that has learned one new trade and cannot yet pay a man to do it with.**

**OF THE THIRTY-FIVE LOCKS IN `outline/volume-05.md` — AND IT IS THIRTY-FIVE, NOT THIRTY-FOUR, BECAUSE L1a IS A LOCK WITH ITS OWN HEADING AND BINDS EVERY LATER VOLUME — NONE IS BREACHED. ONE IS NOT PAID AND IS CARRIED OPEN (L7). SEVEN ARE HELD IN A FORM NARROWER OR BROADER THAN THE LOCK ASKED FOR AND EACH IS RULED (L1, L3, L11, L15, L21, L23, L28). TWENTY-SEVEN HOLD EXACTLY.**

## Volume 06, Batch 0001 (Chapters 251–260) — Movement 1, an instrument that is not there, and a boat that is not the yard's

**WRITTEN 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FROM `outline/volume-06.md` AND `outline/batches/volume-06-batch-0001.md`, BOTH CREATED BY THE PHASE THAT WROTE THEM, AND REPAIRED THE SAME DAY AGAINST `logs/next.review.log`. 35,060 WORDS ACROSS TEN CHAPTERS AFTER THE REPAIRS** (Ch251 3,541 · Ch252 3,251 · Ch253 2,959 · Ch254 3,128 · Ch255 3,107 · Ch256 3,847 · Ch257 3,854 · Ch258 3,466 · Ch259 3,544 · Ch260 4,363), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline, and the batch closed on 34,802 words (Ch251 3,530 · Ch252 3,207 · Ch253 2,959 · Ch254 3,116 · Ch255 3,107 · Ch256 3,825 · Ch257 3,850 · Ch258 3,452 · Ch259 3,541 · Ch260 4,215). **THREE CHAPTERS ARE INSIDE IT AND SEVEN ARE PAST IT, CHAPTER 252 BEING PAST IT BY FIFTY-ONE WORDS AFTER THE REPAIRS AND BY SEVEN BEFORE THEM, AND THE LARGEST IS CHAPTER 260 AT 4,363, AND THE REASON IS ON THE FACE OF THE RECORD: 260 is the batch's close and carries the whole standing inventory once, as `chapter-0250.md` carried it at 5,252 at the end of Volume 05, and 256, 257 and 259 each carry a full three-column apparatus behind a scene of about two thousand words. NO SCENE WAS CUT AND NO COST WAS DROPPED TO BRING ANY CHAPTER INSIDE A NUMBER.** The batch's apparatus was thinned on purpose against `reviews/volume-05/volume-05-close.md`'s ruling that the house's length rule was measuring the house's own apparatus, and at 41.5% of the batch's words the apparatus is still above that audit's 41.0% for Volume 05 and is **not** presented as an improvement.

**THE GRID IS FIXED BY `outline/volume-06.md` LOCK L1 AND NOT BY AN ALMANAC: the fifteenth of March next is a Monday, the batch opens Tuesday the sixteenth and closes Sunday the fourth of April, twenty consecutive days, two days to a chapter, no day missing and no day in two chapters, and no chapter states a year.** The slate wall came down bare twice, being the forty-sixth on the twenty-second of March and the forty-seventh on the twenty-ninth. The cistern was charged five times, on the eighteenth and twenty-fifth of March and the first of April, and on the twenty-second and twenty-ninth of March, and was walked on none of them, being the thirtieth, thirty-first, thirty-second, thirty-third and thirty-fourth of the charges walked on none since the third of August. The three wage Tuesdays of the batch are the sixteenth, the twenty-third and the thirtieth of March, and the weeks fallen due with no envelope went from twenty to twenty-one, twenty-two and twenty-three, and **the wage column did not move and the twenty envelopes did not move on any of them.**

### Chapter 251 — *The Drawer With Six Papers In It and a Place for One That Is Not There*

**Tuesday the sixteenth and Wednesday the seventeenth of March. The shed, and a boat of about forty tons at the face of the second seam.** The ship's hand counts the drawer twice and reads the form of the fourth of March from the top to the bottom and finds that the whole of it asks this yard for nothing, and then does the arithmetic nobody in that shed wanted to do: a hirer at the second seam pays for a piece of paper, and the piece of paper is in a drawer, and the drawer is empty of it. **For the first time since the sixth of October a working day cannot be booked at all, and the twenty-first week falls due on a Tuesday on which no envelope is made.** The boat is hired at a mark and eightpence on the Wednesday because the man of the second lay will not go out past a fouled mark with a hand short, and at the mark there are two bright places on the shackle the size of a thumbnail. Cost: 168p out, nothing in, and a day booked for nothing. Opens the mark thread, which is the batch's spine.

### Chapter 252 — *Six Marks a Day and a Man of About Forty, and the Terms of a Boat That Is Not the Yard's*

**Thursday the eighteenth and Friday the nineteenth of March. The mole's steps and the shed. The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none.** Tobias Wren, sixty-one, half deaf, who has not been on a page since Volume 02, comes up the steps because he heard on Saturday at a fish market that a mark was fouled, and says he did not come to be asked. **He asks one question — who is paying — and names his own terms before any of them is asked for: six marks a day for the boat whatever the month, a hand of about forty called Ruad paid separate by the day, and fuel at what fuel costs; and he says he will not be a teacher, and that in thirty-one years by another man's account he was never asked what was in a thing he went into and has never said.** Cost: nothing out and nothing in, and the price of the boat of six marks a day entered in the second column as **twice** what a day of work on the second seam pays, being nine hundred and sixty pence against four hundred and eighty. **This figure was recorded here as corrected on the day the batch closed and had not been corrected in the chapter; `logs/next.review.log` found it still wrong in the dialogue and in the in-scene second column, and it is now twice in all three places and agrees with the apparatus and with the card file.**

### Chapter 253 — *Nineteen Days, and a Bench at the Far End of the Shed He Did Not Go To*

**Saturday the twentieth and Sunday the twenty-first of March. The shed and the top of the mole.** The holder of that writ comes up the coast road in another man's cart, nineteen days after he went up it, thinner, with a forearm that has a shape to it, and reads the form of the fourth of March in four minutes standing and says there is nothing in it he could not have said out loud on a mole. **He brings back four lines in a hand that is not his own and not hers, being two of a place, one of a month nobody here uses, and one sentence, and the sentence is that four people will not send a man to a coast where the man who writes things down is the one everybody remembers; and that he asked them once, for a witness alive who had been on that water, and was refused three times before the sentence was read to him.** The far bench is occupied on the Sunday from ten until four and he does not go over, and says the reason out loud to a doorframe and asks that the words go in the book and not the reason. Cost: four days lost to the body on the road, entered in the second column with no figure.

### Chapter 254 — *The Forty-Sixth Time the Wall Came Down, and the Shackle That Was Bright*

**Monday the twenty-second and Tuesday the twenty-third of March. The reeve's bench on the west basin, and the water at the face of the second seam.** The wall behind the reeve's bench comes down bare for the forty-sixth time since the first of May, the counter does not restart, and the clerk says that the standing instruction is a wipe every Monday at eight whether there is a sitting or not, and that he has a page of dates and would not give it to anybody who had not asked. **The twenty-second week falls due on the Tuesday and the column does not move; the holder offers to take the slate and is refused by the ship's hand on the ground that a private hire of a boat is not a place to put a man in the water, and he says so first and asks for it entered.** On the water: three bright places on the shackle and not two, the Wednesday's two not struck out, a shackle that has been off the frame and back on the wrong way up, and a wire cut and mended with three turns on the water side of a frame where this yard turns on the landward side. Cost: 169p out. Opens the make.

### Chapter 255 — *What Fouling Says, and the Four People Who Are Not Going to Be Asked*

**Wednesday the twenty-fourth and Thursday the twenty-fifth of March. The mole's steps, the shed, and the head of that dock. The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none.** A man of the second lay with nineteen years in the water reads the slate and gives four things in order — a season for growth, bright iron for a hand inside a fortnight, a spanner and a morning for a mended cut, and **that none of those three knows a name and anybody who says which of four people did it is selling** — and then says all four of them out loud at the head of that dock on the Thursday in eleven people's hearing, and asks that it be written down that he said no word about the four people four days down that coast. He also says that a mark with two parties in it gets a wire through in the dark by a man with no lights. Cost: nothing out, nothing in. Pays the limit for the fourth time and makes the standing refusal audible in a street.

### Chapter 256 — *A Line and a Shackle of a Make Nobody Here Makes, and a Leaf With Four Columns*

**Friday the twenty-sixth and Saturday the twenty-seventh of March. The water and the shed.** The fouling is a foot where on the twenty-third there had been about four inches, **which is eight inches in three days, and that is the rule the man of the second lay builds his bucket out of and says out loud**, there is a four-strand untarred line on the mark tied with a sheet bend, and the shackle has a quarter-inch flat on the crown of the bow that the sea does not make — **and the ship's hand knows the make because she has made the other one, and the man of the second lay says there is one smith on this coast who finishes them that way and that he is sixty-eight and has a shop off the yard at Ternwatch.** Three things are said out loud with their prices before any of them is agreed: a cut, which wants a boat and a man and an instrument this yard has none of; a bigger mark, at four marks of iron, which is the only one it could pay for and the one it does not want; and a complaint, which is a name in a book in a town four days down that coast and is the four people of that coast. **The holder wants the complaint, is refused, loses the argument to his own sentence of the eleventh of March, and asks that it be entered that he lost it to his own sentence and not to a better one.** Saturday: a fourth leaf is ruled on the back of a sheet of chandler's paper with four columns — what was there, who saw it, what was done, and what it is not — the fourth head written with a blunt nib, and it goes on no wall. Cost: 168p out. The batch's turn in the page.

### Chapter 257 — *The Question at the Mole Head, and Three Facts That Are Not an Answer*

**Sunday the twenty-eighth and Monday the twenty-ninth of March. The head of that dock, and the reeve's bench. The wall comes down bare for the forty-seventh time and the cistern is charged and walked on none.** A man of about thirty-one of the third lay with a coaster out of Ashtide puts a Crown chart on the fish quay where anybody can get at it, and says the plate calls the ground the remains of a fleet lost in a sea catastrophe and a matter of record and not of dispute, and asks whether the fleet was scuttled or whether it went down, and gives his own small reason for wanting to know. **The holder of that writ answers, badly, and knows he is answering badly: three things, said out loud in that order, and a statement before them that none of them is an answer — that there is nobody in this harbor old enough, that the only thing that could answer it is at seventy fathoms about four hundred miles west-southwest of that mole and its name is not going to be said in a market, and that this party holds no instrument of any kind.** The man asks one question, being whether the thirty-eighth line will be there in four years, and gets the honest answer and calls it the answer. Nothing of it is written down, and the reason is entered.

### Chapter 258 — *The Twenty-Third Week, and a Season That Ends Tomorrow With Nothing Issued*

**Tuesday the thirtieth and Wednesday the thirty-first of March. The shed, and the reeve's office on the west basin.** The twenty-third week falls due on the Tuesday and the column does not move, and it is the whole of the list of that year. The reeve gives three things and says before giving them that a man who is given three things has not been refused three things: **no instrument after that day, the ledger being spent; that this office is not the office that can give the charter back and never was, said a third time because a true sentence does not wear out; and the one thing it can do, which is to seat two people and take a finding as it is given, at a cost of two clerks and himself, one afternoon a week, in a room he has to be warm for.** The yard asks who the other side is and is told that the other side is a man of fifty of the second lay and a smith in Ternwatch who has never heard of the yard, and that seating either of them as a party would be inventing a proceeding to get at a name. **The reeve says out loud that he is not a friend of that party and that this is not a rescue, and the reason he says it is so that the story is harder to tell in a month.** Nothing is written to the tide gang and nothing to the man of the second lay.

### Chapter 259 — *A Fireman of a Pressure House, and the Condition That Nothing Goes Under That Has Not Been Cut*

**Thursday the first and Friday the second of April. The shed and the top of that mole. The cistern is charged on the Thursday and walked on none, being the thirty-fourth, and the third lay fills at seven in the morning with one berth short of two.** **Garrow Fisk, thirty-four, of a pressure house at Ternwatch, comes up the hill with that yard's printed sheet folded at the thirty-eighth line and says he is not here about a job and wants to know whether that line is true; and is told that it is.** He then offers air and a housing and a man off a Crown house book at a Crown house rate, refuses to pretend this party is a Crown hull, sets one condition — **he will not go under anything that has not been cut, and that is not a rule in a book but what happened to a man he worked beside for nine years who is alive and does not go down at all now** — and names two marks and eightpence a day for his own time before he is asked. The ship's hand writes it all and reads it back twice and he corrects two words. Asked who cuts, the yard says it has not got a cutter, and the man sits on the top step for an hour and a half and is brought out a cup of water and no more. **Nobody in that shed names the only man in that harbor who could, and the not-naming is entered in the second column with a reason about the trade and not about a family: a man asked on a mole whether he cuts answers it with a price, and this yard cannot pay one and is not going to ask a man of sixty-one what he will take.** Cost: a day lost to the body, and a Sunday berth in the third lay that went to a boat out of the north basin because a man was at the head of that dock being answered.

### Chapter 260 — *Two Letters Crossing, and a Party That Has Become One Address More*

**Saturday the third and Sunday the fourth of April. The shed, and the post on the corner.** **The batch's turn.** A letter of one page, asking nothing, goes to the ordinary post at a penny for a man of about thirty who cannot read in a town two days down that coast: what was found on the mark on three named dates **and which finding belongs to which of them, being two bright places and four inches on the seventeenth, a third bright place on the underside and a wire cut and mended on the twenty-third, and the foreign line and the quarter-inch flat on the twenty-sixth**, that the copy of the sheet read out once in a kitchen about nine people has not been read out since and is not going to be, and that whoever put it there did a thing this yard cannot undo. **The holder of that writ does not sign it, and gives two reasons, the second of which is that a name on a piece of paper about a mark that two parties have a claim to, going out of this yard to a town two days down that coast and near a town four days down it where four people keep a page in a drawer, is this yard's name in an office's file in about two years; the letter is signed at the foot in the hand of the man of the second lay.** The letter that came up the hill on the Friday from Mara Venn says one fact and asks for nothing: an office she worked in for nine years has a form for material it calls unsettled, a person who keeps unsettled material in a house is a keeper of it under that form, whether that means a house or a drawer or a cupboard the form does not say, she has not seen it served and cannot say what it does, and she will come if a person asks her and not if a yard asks her. **Nothing was asked of her, nothing was written back, and not writing back is entered in the second column and not the first, because the first column is a record of what a man did.** Sunday: the far bench is occupied from ten until four and is not approached; the fourth leaf has one entry in its first column and one line in its fourth, being **the fourteenth of May, which is nine weeks from the day the mark was set, a date a man can go and look at a mark and see whether it is true, and a date on which nobody in that shed expects to be able to pay a man to go and look.** The batch closes on that figure and on a bag of sixty-five marks and threepence.

### The money, re-derived and closed in one line

**THE WHOLE OF THIS BATCH CLOSES IN ONE LINE: 11,237 − 168 − 169 − 168 − 328 − 1 = 10,403.** The bag opens on seventy marks, three shillings and one penny and closes on sixty-five marks and threepence, being ten thousand four hundred and three pence, and the figure the bag is short of the wage of four marks is nine thousand seven hundred and sixty-three pence. **The whole of what came into that yard in the twenty days is NOTHING, and that is the first twenty-day run in five volumes in which nothing came in at all, and the reason is a form that came up a mole in a hand on the fourth of March and not a mood.** The whole of what went out is eight hundred and thirty-four pence, being three days of the boat of the man of the second lay at one mark and eightpence with a penny of line, two marks and eightpence to a man of a pressure house at Ternwatch, and a penny of postage. The whole of what has gone out of that yard since the sixth of December past becomes **eight marks, six shillings and two pence in eleven transactions at five counters.** Every reading of the bag in the ten chapters is the correct rendering of the marks, shillings and pence beside it.

### The eleven things a Volume 06 writer must not re-derive wrongly

1. **THE CONDITION IS NAMED NOWHERE IN VOLUME 06 AND THE COUNT IS ZERO.** The body is charged: four days lost on the coast road in Chapter 253 and one day lost in Chapter 259, both in the second column with a reason against the line and no name in it, and a day lost carries no figure because there is no figure for a day. The four nameless lines above the named line of the twentieth of July are still four and are in about sixty boats.
2. **THERE ARE NO RETURNS IN THESE TWENTY DAYS.** Four returns stand and there is no fifth, and no finding of worked glass was made, nothing was raised, no person of this yard was in any water on any of the twenty days, and no return of any kind was made out of any of them.
3. **THE PLATE NOTE IS NOT PAID. IT IS PART AND CARRIED OPEN FOR A THIRD VOLUME, AND THE ONLY MECHANISM ANYBODY HAS NAMED FOR REACHING IT IS A CHANGE OF POLITICS IN AN OFFICE AND NOT A BETTER LETTER OUT OF A SHED — WHICH IS A SENTENCE ON THE PAGE FROM VOLUME 05 AND IS NOT IMPROVED ON IN ANY OF THESE TEN CHAPTERS.**
4. **THE FOURTH LEAF IS NEW, IS RULED ON SATURDAY THE TWENTY-SEVENTH OF MARCH, MAY NOT BE DATED EARLIER, MAY NOT GO ON ANY WALL IN MORROW QUAY, AND IS A PAGE NO MAN CAN CHECK.** Four columns: what was there, who saw it, what was done, and what it is not. It has one entry in its first column and one line in its fourth, **and that line is the fourteenth of May, which is nine weeks from the twelfth of March, and it is the only figure in the fourth column in the whole batch.**
5. **THE THREE FIGURES THAT MUST NOT BE SWAPPED ARE NOT STATED IN THIS BATCH AND WERE NOT ADVANCED, AND THE OFFICE'S THREE HUNDRED YEARS WAS NOT HANDED TO THE COMMUNITIES.** The volume's own question is different from Volume 05's and neither was answered.
6. **THE COMPARTMENT IS SPENT, THE THREE GANNET DOCUMENTS ARE STILL THREE, THE NINE-LINE MARK IS NOT RE-OPENED, AND THE DELAYED NOTE STANDS AT ELEVEN AND TWO WITH NOTHING MEASURED AND NO OCCASION ENTERED.**
7. **THE FAR BENCH IS OCCUPIED ON THREE DAYS OF THE TWENTY, BEING THE NINETEENTH, THE TWENTY-FIRST AND THE TWENTY-SIXTH OF MARCH, AND NOT OCCUPIED AT ANY HOUR ON THE OTHER SEVENTEEN, AND THE HOLDER DID NOT GO OVER TO HIM ON ANY OF THE TWENTY.** He was not asked for anything on any of the twenty days and asked nothing on any of them, and there was no fragment and no report talk and no second story, and nothing was put on that bench and read as a gift.
8. **THE CHANDLER'S NOTE STANDS AT THIRTY-ONE, NOT RE-WRITTEN, ITS THREE COUNTS OF WEEKS UNMOVED AT TWENTY-TWO, THIRTEEN AND ELEVEN, AND THE REFUSAL OF A FIFTH SAYING STANDS UNREVERSED.**
9. **NO POWER STAGE IS TAKEN IN VOLUME 06. THE FAULTREADER STAGE IS SPENT AND WAS NOT RE-TAKEN, IMPROVED OR RENAMED. THE BELLWRIGHT STAGE WAS TAKEN IN VOLUME 05 AND IS NOT IMPROVED.** A seam identifies difference and not truth and settled nothing in this batch.
10. **THE QUIETENING AND THE CROWN SEAL ARE NAMED NOWHERE IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS, AND NEITHER IS THE OFFICE OF RETURNING MEMORY, AND NO ONE OF THE NINE FINAL ROUTES IS NAMED IN THE PROSE.** The name of the place at seventy fathoms is refused in a market in Chapter 257 and is not on a page of the yard's book.
11. **THE HOUSE'S LENGTH RULE IS STILL MEASURING THE HOUSE'S OWN APPARATUS AND THE BATCH IS NOT AN EXCEPTION.** 34,802 words across ten chapters as the batch closed and 35,060 after the review repairs, an average of 3,506, against Volume 05's 4,165 a chapter. Three chapters inside the guideline and seven past it, Chapter 252 past it by fifty-one words after the repairs and by seven before them, and the batch's apparatus at 41.5% of its words is inside a point of Volume 05's audited 41.0%, so **the apparatus was thinned on purpose and did not come out materially thinner, and that is recorded here rather than represented as a fix.**

### The volume's shape so far, stated on its own face

**MOVEMENT 1 OF VOLUME 06 IS COMPLETE. TWENTY CONSECUTIVE DAYS FROM TUESDAY THE SIXTEENTH OF MARCH TO SUNDAY THE FOURTH OF APRIL, TWO DAYS TO A CHAPTER, NO DAY MISSING AND NO DAY IN TWO CHAPTERS, NO CHAPTER STATES A YEAR, AND NO DIVE AND NO PERSON OF THIS YARD IN ANY WATER ON ANY OF THE TWENTY DAYS. THE VOLUME'S QUESTION WAS ASKED AT A MOLE HEAD AND WAS NOT ANSWERED; THE VOLUME'S CENTRAL PRESSURE IS A PARTY WITH NO INSTRUMENT OF ANY KIND; THE BATCH'S TURN IS A FOURTH COLUMN AND A LETTER; AND THE VOLUME'S CLOSING POSITION FOR THE TWENTY DAYS IS THAT A WORKING HARBOR IS POORER AND THAT A YARD WHICH HAS BEEN TWENTY DAYS WITHOUT A PAPER HAS SENT ONE LETTER AND RECEIVED ONE.**

## Volume 06, Batch 0001 — review repairs, 27 September 2026, against `logs/next.review.log`

**FIFTEEN FINDINGS, SEVEN SERIOUS AND EIGHT MODERATE, DISPOSED OF IN THE CHAPTERS THAT CARRIED THEM AND NOT BY REWRITING A CHAPTER. NO SUMMARY BELOW IS REWRITTEN; THE FIGURES THAT MOVED ARE CORRECTED IN PLACE AND THE WORD COUNTS AT THE HEAD OF THIS SECTION ARE BOTH ON THE RECORD. Finding by finding, `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0001 (Volume 06) review repairs*.**

1. **251 — the first column said the boat was hired at two marks and the scene said a mark and eightpence. It is one mark and eightpence in both.** The mark is **five days old** on the seventeenth and the ship's miscount of it stays in the scene as a character's error and is struck out of the record.
2. **252 — the man of sixty-one who asked is now a man of sixty-one of this harbor, and not *of this yard*.** A six-marks-a-day boat is **twice** a day's work on the second seam, in the dialogue, in the in-scene second column and in the apparatus, and the reply no longer corrects an arithmetic error.
3. **253 — unchanged in word and figure. Nineteen days, four lines, a bench, and no going over.**
4. **254 — the mark is eleven days old and there are about four inches of green on it, and four inches in eleven days is not a season's fouling.** The fouling is not three days old and never was.
5. **255 — unchanged. Four things, eleven people, and the limit four people of that coast wrote and signed, which is four days down that coast.**
6. **256 — the cistern is not charged on Friday the twenty-sixth, which was never a rota day, and the reason against the line of no day booked is the party of two in a hired boat with no paper to book it against.** The fouling is a foot where the twenty-third had four inches, being **eight inches in three days**, and that is the rule the man of the second lay builds and says out loud.
7. **257 — the four people who keep the book of ground are four days down that coast, and not four days up.** The man of thirty-one came up on **Friday the twenty-sixth**. The counted reference to the length of this book is now "four times in five volumes".
8. **258 — the reeve is sitting on the third thing since the fourth of March and the second wait is the same wait, said once.** The holder says yes to the Monday and the offer still dies on who the other side is, and nothing is written to the tide gang or to the man of the second lay.
9. **259 — the not-naming's reason is about the trade: a man asked on a mole whether he cuts answers it with a price, and this yard cannot pay one and is not going to ask a man of sixty-one what he will take.** The distance between two benches and a man's own son are both out of it.
10. **260 — the letter keeps its three dates apart: two bright places and four inches on the seventeenth, a third bright place on the underside and a cut wire on the twenty-third, a foreign line and a quarter-inch flat on the twenty-sixth.** The town the letter goes to is two days down and the four people with the page in a drawer are four days down and the two are not the same place. The penny is the whole of what the yard spent in **one** day, and the figure before it was two marks and eightpence on the Friday. The twenty-day outgoings are **five marks, two shillings and ten pence**. The mark is **twenty-three days old**, and the fourth leaf's only line in its fourth column is **the fourteenth of May**.

## Volume 06, Batch 0002 (Chapters 261–270) — Movement 2, the air, and the only man on this coast who will cut it

**WRITTEN 27 SEPTEMBER 2026 FROM `outline/volume-06.md` AND THE FOUR STATE FILES, AND REPAIRED 28 SEPTEMBER 2026 AGAINST `logs/batch-0002.review.log` WITHOUT BEING RESTARTED. NO CARD FILE EXISTS FOR THIS BATCH AND NONE WAS INVENTED; `outline/batches/` STILL HOLDS `volume-06-batch-0001.md` AND NOTHING ELSE FOR VOLUME 06. 31,660 WORDS ACROSS TEN CHAPTERS AFTER THOSE REPAIRS** (Ch261 3,720 · Ch262 2,830 · Ch263 3,250 · Ch264 2,896 · Ch265 2,927 · Ch266 3,556 · Ch267 2,538 · Ch268 3,214 · Ch269 2,844 · Ch270 3,885), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline, and 30,154 words (Ch261 3,717 · Ch262 2,830 · Ch263 2,680 · Ch264 2,765 · Ch265 2,847 · Ch266 2,920 · Ch267 2,750 · Ch268 3,081 · Ch269 2,836 · Ch270 3,728) as it first closed. **FIVE CHAPTERS ARE INSIDE IT AND FIVE ARE PAST IT, AND THE LARGEST IS CHAPTER 270 AT 3,885, WHICH IS THE BATCH'S CLOSE AND CARRIES THE WHOLE STANDING INVENTORY ONCE, as `chapter-0260.md` carried it at 4,363. NO SCENE WAS CUT AND NO COST WAS DROPPED TO BRING ANY CHAPTER INSIDE A NUMBER.** The batch's apparatus was thinned on purpose: **Batch 0001 carried the whole standing inventory in every one of its ten chapters, and this one carries it whole in Chapter 270 and nowhere else, and the first-column and second-column entries in the other nine do dated work instead of restating the book — which is now true of Chapter 267 as well, and was not.** The apparatus is 41.2% of the batch's words after the repairs against 40.9% for it as first written, and the whole disposition of the review is at `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0002 (Volume 06) review repairs*. Measured on the method `reviews/volume-05/volume-05-close.md` used — every paragraph split at the markers *First column* and *Second column*, the words in each class counted and the remainder counted as scene — **the apparatus is 40.9% of this batch's words against 41.5% for Batch 0001 and 41.0% for Volume 05, and that figure is recorded here and is not presented as a fix, because the Volume 05 audit ruled that the house's length rule is measuring the house's own apparatus and not its prose.**

**THE GRID IS FIXED BY `outline/volume-06.md` LOCK L1 AND NOT BY AN ALMANAC: the fifteenth of March next is a Monday, the batch opens Monday the fifth of April and closes Wednesday the fourteenth, ten consecutive days, one day to a chapter, no day missing and no day in two chapters, and no chapter states a year. ONE RULE OF THAT LOCK IS DEPARTED FROM AND THE DEPARTURE IS RECORDED RATHER THAN CONCEALED — see `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0002 as written*, item 1.** The slate wall came down bare twice, being the forty-eighth on the fifth of April and the forty-ninth on the twelfth. The cistern was charged on three rota days, the fifth, the eighth and the twelfth, and was walked on none of them, being the thirty-fifth, thirty-sixth and thirty-seventh of the charges walked on none since the third of August. The two wage Tuesdays of the batch are the sixth and the thirteenth of April, and the weeks fallen due with no envelope went from twenty-three to twenty-four and twenty-five, and **the wage column did not move and the twenty envelopes did not move on either of them.**

### Chapter 261 — *The Rate, Said Out Loud at the Head of That Mole, and the Cut on a Bench Above the Water*

**Monday the fifth of April. The head of that mole and the shed.** The wall behind the reeve's bench comes down bare for the forty-eighth time and the cistern is charged at no figure and walked on none, being the thirty-fifth. **Garrow Fisk names the figure he would not give on the second of April: the compressor, the housing, the pipe, the gauges and one man off a Crown house book are nine marks a day, his own time is two marks and eightpence a day, a hand on the hose is a mark and two shillings and not a penny more, and three days of it is thirty-six marks and eight shillings, a day at twelve marks, two shillings and eightpence, and he says it on the mole steps in about nine people's hearing and nobody in that hearing said it was small.** He then says why his condition is not caution but a line: everything he sells is on the end of a line, and when there is nothing hung on the bottom the line is a rope, and when there is something hung on it the line is a thing a swell can take hold of, and what he wants is for the line to be short enough that it cannot be used against the man on it. **Nessa Pike makes the cut in a sentence — a bight of shot line through a shackle, the two ends on the crown, the bight cut on the surface when the pot is on the bottom — and then makes it wrong on purpose, turning the three turns on the water side against that yard's own practice, because a shackle on the face of the second seam carries three turns on the water side and a shackle of this yard's own making would be two and two for a man with a spanner.** And **Elias Voke, fifty-one, of the *Anna Ray* of the second lay, comes up with his net hung on the bottom of the cut and a mile and a half of ground shut against six boats, and offers eleven marks before any price has been spoken of, and says he will not be argued up.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out. Establishes the rate card, the cut, the payer and the fourth column's second line.

### Chapter 262 — *Twenty-Four Weeks on a Tuesday, and the First Figure That Has Come Into That Yard in Twenty-Three Days*

**Tuesday the sixth of April. The shed.** The twenty-fourth week falls due and the column does not move. **Voke puts eleven marks on the bench in threes and fives and a great many ones and it is counted twice and read back and counted back by him, and it is the first money that has come into that yard since the fourteenth of March.** He asks the one question he came to ask, which is who comes up if it goes wrong, and is told that is a question about a price and not about a man. **The holder of that writ corrects his own sentence of the thirtieth of March in the open — twenty-three was the whole of that year's list to the thirtieth day of March, and the list has a week in it for this morning — and asks that the old sentence not be struck out.** The set is engaged for the eighth, ninth and tenth at nine marks a day, and Fisk says out loud that he wrote the yard's name on a sheet and not in the Crown house book, and that a Crown house book has a column for a depth and no column for a year, a name or a reason, and enters that himself, unsigned. And **the form for unsettled material enters as legal pressure: this yard became an address in the ordinary post on the third of April, and an address is a thing that can be given to a man who is going somewhere, and the decision not to write to the woman who wrote on the second is entered in the second column and not the first.** Cost: 1,760p in, nothing out.

### Chapter 263 — *The Cut Made on a Bench Above the Water, and the Book That Is Not There*

**Wednesday the seventh of April. The bench outside the shed, the boat, and the crown of the mole.** **A shackle of that yard's own iron is swaged and closed cold with a drift and three blows of a sledge, and the three turns go on the water side, and the serving goes on half a turn out of true, and that fault is entered in the fourth column and not the first because a thing which is not is not a thing a man did.** A man of twenty-six named Fen Barrow is hired at a mark and two shillings a day and holds the bight while the turns are made. **The man of the second lay asks how far off the ground is and Fisk answers it before he has finished asking: the outer marks are a mile and a quarter off the head of that mole, the bar is four hundred yards off them and begins about a mile and a half out on the north-west line, and the cut is off the south end and round the corner from all of it, and nobody has narrowed that bar and this party is not going to ask, and he asks for it to be said again in the boat and before the two men go down, and the holder of that writ says it goes in the first column with the day and the price on it and not on its own, and Fisk says nothing is on its own.** **The six conditions of a first entry are read aloud at the boat in order, and there is no first entry and nothing to enter, and the holder of that writ then says out loud in front of five people that there is no harbor authority's book to go in — the ledger is spent, the season ended on the thirty-first of March, and no instrument has been issued since — and the ship's hand answers that that yard's own book is not that office's book and is not the same thing and cannot be the same thing, and `outline/volume-06.md` lock L22 is paid on the page and not renamed.** The pot is charged to **eighty-four pounds on a face of a hundred and fifty** by a gauge a man can see the needle of, and eighty-four is not near the end of it, and a pencil mark is left on the face where the needle stood, which is what lets a man in a shed at Ternwatch and a man in this shed put the two documents side by side, and the reading is entered in the yard's book in a hand that is not the ship's and is unsigned. **And with this figure the sentence the yard has carried since the third week of March — the figure the bag is short of the wage of four marks — comes to an end, and it comes to an end because there is no longer any such figure and not because anything was paid.** Cost: 172p out.

### Chapter 264 — *The Forty Fathoms, and the Floor That Is Not the Floor of a Quarry*

**Thursday the eighth of April. Off the south end and a mile and a half out.** The cistern is charged and walked on none, being the thirty-sixth. **A day of work is done in that yard and is not booked, because there is no paper in the drawer to book it against, and the day is in the first column with the price named before it was agreed, and there is no first entry and nothing to enter.** The set goes out on the top of the flood; **Amos Searle, twenty-eight, a holder four years off a Crown house book, takes the shot line and the two ends of the bight are cut on the surface at about eleven while a man is forty fathoms down, and nothing on the surface of the earth is then holding anything at all.** At forty fathoms the bottom is not the floor of a quarry: it is a laid floor of cut stone in courses running in straight lines for about a hundred and forty feet, **with a figure cut in the stone at the near end of every course, cut before the stone was set, running from one to forty-one without a break and then past a hundred beyond a turn in the trench**, and in a socket in the top course there is a bar of square iron and a chain made to the bar and Voke's net hung across thirty feet of the floor. **The holder of that writ comes up with a figure he cannot get out of his hand, and nobody asks him what he means, and the reason is in the second column.** Cost: 1,952p out, being nine marks for the set, two marks and eightpence for Fisk and a mark and two shillings for the hand. The running outgoings since the sixth of December are twenty-one marks, nine shillings and tenpence in thirteen transactions at six counters.

### Chapter 265 — *A Net Does Not Feel Like a Net at Forty Fathoms in the Dark*

**Friday the ninth of April. The same water, and the shed.** The warp comes off the bar in fifty minutes and the bar comes out of its socket, **and then the net comes up, slow and wide and all at once, and takes Amos Searle up about eleven feet and puts him into the edge of a course of squared stone.** He says one thing and asks for it in his own words: a net that has been hung eleven days comes up slow, and if he had known that in the morning he would have gone under it and not beside it, and he knew it in the morning and did not think about it, and it is not anybody's fault. **Fisk says on the crown of the mole in about a hundred people's hearing that the shoulder is not out and is gone, and that the book at Ternwatch carries the man as unfit for five weeks from the ninth of April and as fit for light work after, and that the word light is his house's word and that he has never been able to make a man light.** The day is a waste and everybody says so, and **Nessa Pike refuses to put a hopeful figure in the second column, and Fisk then supplies the real one: the bed at Ashtide is a shilling a day and a week has been paid because a week is the week the yard hopes for, and the second week is not that yard's because that yard has not got a second week.** Cost: 1,952p out, and a man five weeks and a rib. **Fisk says he will read the entry out of the book when it comes up the road and not one minute before, and the book went down that road on the Thursday night with the news of the shoulder in it and came up again on the Friday night.** Running outgoings: thirty-three marks, twelve shillings and sixpence in fourteen transactions at six counters.

### Chapter 266 — *The Third Day Without a Holder, a Bar of Iron, and a Bill at Ashtide*

**Saturday the tenth of April. The same water, the shed, and the post on the corner.** **A Crown house sent a man: Hesk Ottrim, thirty-one, up on the Friday night in a coach that took fourteen hours, at two marks and eightpence a day, and he says out loud in a boat that he asked for the job.** **And at about seven that morning, before the boat went out, Fisk reads the Crown house book out in that shed with the door open and about four people in the street — the book came up the coast road on the Friday night in the same coach and went down on the Thursday night with the news of the shoulder in it, and the road is a night's road each way — being the man of twenty-eight carried as unfit for five weeks from the ninth of April, then as fit for light work only, then as fit on the fifth of June, and the word light is his house's word and there is no light work in a holder's trade, and the ship's hand enters the four lines in the second column in his words and the figure in no column.** The day costs fourteen marks, three shillings and fourpence, and **Voke gets two hundred and eight fathoms of net out of four hundred and eleven, with eleven of eleven floats and the head rope entire, and the belly is still on the floor with a chain round it, and he says he has lost the part that was the money and does not say thank you.** Fisk names his travel at one mark and fourpence a day for six days, being six marks and two shillings, and counts it out on the front step in front of about eleven people, and says the fortnight before was his own and is not going on that book. **The bill goes out as three separate figures — a boat to a hospital at Ashtide with a man in her at one mark and eightpence, the man's fee at four marks, and a week's keep of a bed at seven shillings — and Fisk goes in that boat as far as Ashtide on his own account and says so out loud, and there is no figure for it and there is not going to be one.** And nine feet of chain and a two-foot bar of square iron are in that shed, and every fourth link of the chain is longer than the others by about two inches with an unseized seizing on it. Cost: 2,280p + 984p + 892p out. **Fisk goes in that boat to Ashtide and is away from the eleventh to the fifteenth, saying so out loud on the step.** The running outgoings since the sixth of December are fifty-nine marks, twelve shillings and twopence in seventeen transactions at six counters, and the bag is twenty-four marks, seven shillings and sevenpence and does not move again until the close.

### Chapter 267 — *Nine Feet of Chain, a Boy Who Came Down on His Own Account, and a Day With No Figure Against It*

**Sunday the eleventh of April. The shed and the bench outside it. No Fisk: he went in that boat to Ashtide on the Saturday evening and is back on the Thursday.** The chapter runs in clock order. **The chain is hung on a nail and lies on the floor of that shed in a heap the width of a dinner plate, and a man of about fifty of the second lay looks at it and does not touch it and says it is not chain that came off a boat and that he would not take it if he could, and then asks the ship's hand for the figure in the bag, is given it out loud, is told it is not a third of what the job cost, asks for it to be written on the front of the shed, is refused and is given the reason against the refusal — this yard does not put its figures on a wall, and a man who has asked for one is a man this yard has to go on giving them to — says that is fair, and does not ask again.** **The holder of that writ loses the day to the body**, which is entered with a reason and no name and carries no figure, and stands at the bench outside the shed for the morning with a cup of water and no more. The boy of twenty-two comes down at about eleven on his own account, nobody puts him on anything, the ship's hand reads the whole of the day's entry back including the second column, and **the boy says one thing — the second column is getting full, and it was not getting full in the spring — and goes at about twelve, and is not on the stone on the Monday and has not been asked anything since.** **The fourth column gets its fourth line, dictated at the bench and written by the ship's hand in her own left hand.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out, and the standing inventory is not restated here; it is carried whole in Chapter 270.

### Chapter 268 — *The Forty-Ninth Wipe, a Bearing Taken With a Compass, and a Floor a Mile and a Half Off the South End*

**Monday the twelfth of April. The crown of that mole, and the boat.** The wall comes down bare for the forty-ninth time and the cistern is charged and walked on none, being the thirty-seventh. **The holder of that writ cannot hold a slate and can hold a compass, and a compass is a needle and a card and a bit of looking and is not a join, and that is the first thing he has been able to do in eleven days, and it is entered as a thing that was done and the reason against the other instrument is in the second column with no name in it.** A bearing of **north-west by west, about two hundred and seventy**, is taken four times off the outer end of the crown of the mole, four times along the south side and four times off the head, and the same figure is taken in a boat along the courses on the floor, and **the same figure is the bearing of the crown of the mole on a sheet rolled behind the tide table in that shed which is not a Crown document and is not anybody's document. It is a mile and a half out at forty fathoms and it is a line, and this mole is a line, and they are the same line, and the mole has a foot and the foot is not rubble on a slope. The ground is off the south end and round the corner from the bar of four hundred yards off the outer marks, on the seaward side of it, and the bar has not been narrowed and nobody has asked for it to be.** And then nothing is done about it: nobody is sent for, no letter is written, no figure goes on a wall, and the reason is that a refusal is better than a sheet and that a man going down and being wrong about it is the first worst thing and a chart of a made floor is the second. Fisk then does the arithmetic of the deep water — the floor is at forty fathoms, being two hundred and forty feet, and the needle went to eighty-four on a face of a hundred and fifty, and eighty-four is not near the end of it — and says the third part of it is not a number and is that the set is a pressure house's set on a book and goes where the book sends it, and there is no book on that coast that sends it four hundred miles west and there is no yard that rents one. Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 269 — *Twenty-Five Weeks on a Tuesday, a Man Who Is Not Coming Back, and the Sentence the Whole Thing Was For*

**Tuesday the thirteenth of April. The shed and the head of the mole.** The twenty-fifth week falls due and the column does not move, and the far bench is not occupied, and the man of about thirty-one of the third lay is not asked anything in the ten days and asks nothing. **Voke comes up with the figures he has counted two ways — two hundred and eight fathoms out of four hundred and eleven, being about a half of a season's gear got back and not two fifths of it, the ground shut, the herring off it at the end of next month — and says out loud in a shed that he will not pay the difference and why, and that if he had it he would not give it, because there are other people in that harbor with a boat and a bad season and he has been on nineteen winters of a list and has never once been the man who was carried.** Then the argument the ten days were for, said twice and badly and with both attempts left standing: **the floor at forty fathoms is not about the fleet, and the finding is the method, being that a man laid stone and cut a figure at the end of every course so the man setting it would know whether he had laid a whole course or part of one, and that the same method is at seventy fathoms about four hundred miles west, and that the order a made thing was done in is cut into the made thing and can be read by anybody with a pot and a reason to be in the water — and then this party is the wrong party for it.** The ship's hand puts the two depths and the one bearing in the first column, where a man can check them, and the rest in the second. Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 270 — *The Whole of What It Cost, Read Back From a Leaf With Four Columns, and the Two Halves of One Sentence*

**Wednesday the fourteenth of April. The shed, and the whole standing inventory once.** The leaf is read back in full, all four columns including the empty ones, and it holds **three entries in its first column, seven lines in its second, none in its third, and six in its fourth, being the lines of the fourteenth of May and of the fifth, the seventh, the eleventh, the thirteenth and the fourteenth of April**; the third column is empty, nobody is appointed to it, and the boy's hand is at the head of it. Then the four sentences the ten days were for, in four sentences and no more: **there is no fleet on that floor and there is not going to be, the floor is not the fleet and the floor is not the answer, the floor is the method, and this party is the wrong party for it — and the two halves are one sentence and it is not bad news, because the question is answerable and the answering of it is four hundred miles west under seventy fathoms and everybody who can get there is somebody this yard is not going to ask.** And: the form for unsettled material has never been served on anybody, nobody in this country knows what it does, a form is served by being left at an address, the price of that is a person's afternoon and their fear, and this yard is not going to be the reason. Cost: the whole batch, itemised, and nothing paid twice.

### The batch's money, re-derived from the ten chapters and closing twice

**10,403 + 1,760 − 8,232 = 3,931.** The bag opens at **sixty-five marks and threepence, being ten thousand four hundred and three pence**, and closes at **twenty-four marks, seven shillings and sevenpence, being three thousand nine hundred and thirty-one pence**. **The whole of what came into that yard in the ten days is eleven marks, being one thousand seven hundred and sixty pence from Elias Voke on the sixth of April, and that is also the whole of the money that has come into that yard since the sixth of December past, because nothing came in at all after the fourteenth of March.** Out, in the ten days: **one mark and twelvepence** (the boat of the man of the second lay at one mark and eightpence and four fathoms of line at a penny a fathom, the seventh); **twelve marks, two shillings and eightpence** (the eighth); **twelve marks, two shillings and eightpence** (the ninth); **fourteen marks, three shillings and fourpence** (the tenth, with a second man off the Crown house book); **six marks and two shillings** (Fisk's travel); and **five marks, seven shillings and eightpence** (a boat to a hospital at Ashtide at one mark and eightpence, a man's fee at four marks, and a week's keep of a bed at a shilling the day for seven days). **That is fifty-one marks and six shillings, being eight thousand two hundred and thirty-two pence, and with the eight marks, six shillings and twopence that had gone out since the sixth of December it is fifty-nine marks, twelve shillings and twopence in seventeen transactions at six counters, which is nine thousand five hundred and eighty-six pence.** The running total on the page at each close is **nine marks, seven shillings and twopence in twelve at five** after the seventh, **twenty-one marks, nine shillings and tenpence in thirteen at six** after the eighth, **thirty-three marks, twelve shillings and sixpence in fourteen at six** after the ninth, and the figure above from the tenth onward. **A MARK IS A HUNDRED AND SIXTY PENCE AND A SHILLING IS TWELVE IN THIS BOOK, the hand on the hose is a mark and two shillings a day and a day of the whole set is twelve marks two shillings and eightpence, and every figure above is derivable from those three rates.**

### The volume's shape so far, stated on its own face

**MOVEMENTS 1 AND 2 OF VOLUME 06 ARE WRITTEN AND REPAIRED. THIRTY CONSECUTIVE DAYS FROM TUESDAY THE SIXTEENTH OF MARCH TO WEDNESDAY THE FOURTEENTH OF APRIL, THIRTY DAYS IN TWENTY CHAPTERS, NO DAY MISSING, NO CHAPTER STATES A YEAR, AND THREE DAYS ON WHICH A PERSON OF THAT YARD WENT INTO THE WATER, ALL THREE OF THEM ENTERED IN THE FIRST COLUMN WITH THE REASON AGAINST THE LINE AND THE PRICE NAMED BEFORE IT WAS AGREED, AND THE GROUND OF ALL THREE BEING OFF THE SOUTH END, ON THE SEAWARD SIDE OF THE BAR OF FOUR HUNDRED YARDS OFF THE OUTER MARKS AND NOT INSIDE IT, WITH THE BAR NAMED OUT LOUD AT THE BOAT ON THE SEVENTH AND NOT ASKED TO BE NARROWED. THE VOLUME'S QUESTION IS NOW FOUND TO BE ANSWERABLE AND THIS PARTY IS FOUND TO BE THE WRONG PARTY FOR IT. THE VOLUME'S CLOSING POSITION IS THAT A WORKING HARBOR IS POORER, THAT A YARD HAS BOUGHT THREE DAYS OF A CROWN HOUSE'S AIR AND LOST FORTY MARKS AND SIX SHILLINGS DOING IT, AND THAT IT IS CARRYING A CHAIN ON A NAIL THAT IT CANNOT SELL AND CANNOT GIVE AWAY.**
