NO CARD FILE EXISTS FOR THIS BATCH AND NONE WAS INVENTED; `outline/batches/` STILL HOLDS `volume-06-batch-0001.md` AND NOTHING ELSE FOR VOLUME 06. 27,240 WORDS ACROSS TEN CHAPTERS** (Ch271 2,432 · Ch272 2,277 · Ch273 2,471 · Ch274 2,663 · Ch275 2,339 · Ch276 2,290 · Ch277 2,449 · Ch278 2,972 · Ch279 2,668 · Ch280 4,679), measured with `wc -w` including headings and re-measured after both repair passes of 28 September 2026 recorded at `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0003 (Volume 06) repairs*, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline. **NINE CHAPTERS ARE INSIDE IT AND ONE IS PAST IT, AND THE LARGEST IS CHAPTER 280, WHICH IS THE BATCH'S CLOSE AND CARRIES THE READ-BACK OF NINE DAYS AND THE WHOLE STANDING INVENTORY ONCE, as `chapter-0260.md` carried it at 4,363 and `chapter-0270.md` at 3,885.** The apparatus is **40.6%** of the batch's words by the Volume 05 method, against 41.2% for Batch 0002, 41.5% for Batch 0001 and 41.0% for Volume 05, and it is thinned on purpose: **the whole standing inventory is carried in Chapter 280 and nowhere else, and the first-column and second-column entries in the other nine carry dated figures that the scene also carries rather than restating the book.**

**THE GRID IS FIXED BY `outline/volume-06.md` LOCK L1 AND NOT BY AN ALMANAC: the fifteenth of March next is a Monday, and this batch takes the same shape Batch 0002 took — ten chapters, ten consecutive days, ONE DAY TO A CHAPTER, no day missing, no day in two chapters, and no chapter states a year. THE BATCH RUNS THURSDAY THE FIFTEENTH OF APRIL TO SATURDAY THE TWENTY-FOURTH, and the departure from lock L1 item 5 is the same recorded departure Batch 0002 made and is not re-argued here.** Three cistern rota days fall in it, being the **fifteenth, the nineteenth and the twenty-second of April**, and were walked on none of them, being **the thirty-eighth, thirty-ninth and fortieth** of the charges entered walked on none of since the third of August past. One wall wipe falls in it, being the **fiftieth** on Monday the nineteenth, the counter not restarting and the clerk keeping his own page; the fifty-first is on Monday the twenty-sixth and falls outside the batch. One wage Tuesday falls in it, being the **twentieth**, the twenty-sixth week, and the column did not move on it; the twenty-seventh falls outside the batch.

### Chapter 271 — *The Thirty-Eighth Charge, Four Lines to a Woman Who Wrote First, and a Penny at the Box on the Corner*

**Thursday the fifteenth of April. The shed on the mole, and the box on the corner.** The cistern is charged and walked on none, being the thirty-eighth. **The holder of that writ breaks his own undertaking of Wednesday the fourteenth out loud, in the shed, before nine — all three of the things he said, which were that he would not ask her, would not write to her and would not send anybody — and gives one reason, which is that a form exists, and that a yard which does not answer a letter is an address that answers nothing, and an address is an address, and a yard made itself an address in the ordinary post on the third of April.** He writes four lines, unsigned, and posts them at a penny: she is asked to come, the reason is given on the paper so that it can be argued with for two days before anybody acts on it, and **the fourth line is five words — *I am asking you. Not the yard* — and she reads that line eleven times and the three above it not once.** A day of work is not booked and cannot be, and a drum is beaten out on the mole instead, and there is no figure for an hour and a half with a stick in a drum. Cost: one penny out, and the undertaking of the fourteenth broken on the page and entered as broken.

### Chapter 272 — *A Person of No Office of This Harbor, Thirty, and a Line of the Fourth of April That Is Not Struck Out*

**Friday the sixteenth of April. The shed on the mole, and the mole steps.** She comes up the hill in a cart and pays her own half and says so at the door before anybody asks. **THE GEOGRAPHY CONFLICT IS SETTLED ON THE FACE OF THE PAGE AND NOT INVENTED AWAY: Mara Venn is a person of no office, thirty, OF THIS HARBOR, and has been of this harbor since the fourth of January past, and the second column's line of the fourth of April past which said her letter came four days up that coast IS WRONG, IS NOT STRUCK OUT, and has the said Friday's correction written against it in the second column, and the reason she gives for not striking it is that a yard which strikes out its own line because it was wrong is a yard which has decided that a wrong line is not a record.** She puts a single printed sheet on the bench and squares it with both hands: **a form headed in print as particulars of material held as unsettled, five ruled lines, the first filled in with a name and a place, the place a house in a town two days down that coast, a line at the foot that says served and has nothing after the word, and a seal line with a name on it, and the name is Halven Rook, entered in the first column because a name on a piece of paper is a thing a man can go and look at, and he is in no room.** She **refuses to name the young man in the office who filled in the first line**, and the refusal is entered as refused and not as not-asked, and Calder Marr does not ask twice. And she says out loud that she has had the sheet **eleven weeks, since the fourth of January**, and cannot put it in a drawer and cannot burn it and cannot take it to a post office and cannot hand it to a yard, and that those are four things and she has tried three. Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 273 — *Four Lines, a Wetted Line Cut Away, and an Afternoon at the Face of the Second Seam*

**Saturday the seventeenth of April. The face of the second seam, and the shed.** Thirty-six days on the mark, and it is a foot and three-quarters on the weather side and two inches on the crown, and the shackle cannot be seen and can be found with a thumb, and the flat of a quarter of an inch across its crown is a make this yard does not fit and the four-strand untarred line upon it is not this yard's line. Four hundred and eighty pence goes **out** for that day at that face, because this yard charged itself for it at the rate it has always charged a hirer for a day on that face, with nobody named against it, nobody hired and nobody in the water, and it is not entered against a column. **THE BATCH'S DISCOVERY IS MADE HERE, OUT OF A SHEET THIS YARD ALREADY HAD: the four lines made on the sixth of March past by four people four days down that coast are read out in full and are about a RECORD and not about a HOUSE, there is not one address in them, and the word house is not in them at all. A record may be asked who has read it, which is why the second line is written the way it is written — a no can be asked about, so a no is not written. A house may be asked nothing. A form cannot be served on a record because a record has no address, and a record is given one by writing down where the drawer is, and a house is the only kind of thing in this country you cannot refuse anybody anything about.** And Calder Marr says out loud, once, that **this yard did it on the third of April, in a good cause, and that a page in a drawer in a house is custody that an office can reach, and that the only reason a form has not been at somebody's door in this country is that nobody here has held one, and that that is not a protection, it is a fortnight of luck.** **Nessa Pike refuses to copy the four lines into the fourth column**, and the reason against the line is that copying four lines written by four other people into this yard's own book makes this yard a second hand on a page it does not keep, and this yard is not adding a sixth page to anything. Cost: 480p out.

### Chapter 274 — *Five Doors, One of Which Is a Post Office, and a Penny at the Box on the Corner*

**Sunday the eighteenth of April. The shed, and the box on the corner.** The form is on the bench from nine and **four doors shut on it inside two hours, in this order and entered in this order**: it does not go in the drawer, because a drawer is a drawer and this yard spent Friday finding out what a drawer is, and it will not go on top of a page five people in five towns agreed to keep; it does not go in the tin, **because a tin shuts**; it does not go on a wall, because a sheet on a wall in that harbor is a sheet that goes out of that harbor, and this yard has put four copies of its own sheet out of it already; and it is not burnt, and **the woman of no office says that out loud for the two of them — a person who burns a paper with unsettled material on it has handled it, and a person who has handled it has kept it, and a person who has kept it is the thing the paper is about, so the burning is the keeping and it is the same door** — and that nobody is going to write that down as a rule because a rule in that book is a thing she has to argue with for the rest of her life. **Three addresses are ruled out: this shed, which the woman rules out herself, because a form that can be left on a shed can be left on a room and then on a county and then there is no end to it, and she will not be the wrong place; the boy, in one sentence by the ship's hand, and no name of his is in any column of that book; and a man of about thirty who cannot read in a town two days down that coast, which is the only address in that book which is not that yard's.** And the reason, said out loud and entered without improvement, is **not that he is a keeper — a form cannot tell the difference and neither can the holder of that writ.** It goes in the post at half past four at a penny. **The undertaking of the fourteenth is broken and is entered in the first column as broken and not as kept, and nobody in that shed offered a reason that made it smaller.** Cost: one penny out.

### Chapter 275 — *The Fiftieth Time That Wall Came Down, and an Address a Mile and a Quarter Off the Head of a Mole*

**Monday the nineteenth of April. The shed, and the crown of that mole.** The wall comes down bare for the fiftieth time and the cistern is charged and walked on none, being the thirty-ninth, and the far bench is not occupied at any hour. **The two of them wait from nine to one and there is no figure for waiting and there is not going to be one, and the ship's hand says out loud that this yard has now put three things into the world that it cannot go and look at — the five pages of the sixth of March, the six lines of the fourth column, and a sheet in a post office — and that the third of them is going to arrive on a step and not in a drawer, and that in about four years he is going to want to say that he did not know what was on it, and that he did not, and that the four lines in that column already say so and that saying it out loud in a shed on a Monday is the whole of what saying it out loud in a shed on a Monday does.** Then they stand on the crown for twenty minutes and **see the outer marks for the first time from that ground and take no figure and enter nothing against them**, because the bar of four hundred yards off those marks has not been narrowed and this party is not going to ask to have it narrowed, in advance, in a boat, or on any day. And **a boat of that yard may not go to the outer marks either**, for the same reason and one more: a man of about thirty-one of the third lay has a Crown chart in a bag and would hear of it inside a week. And it is decided in that shed on the same afternoon that **no letter is to be written to the man of about thirty**, because a form on a step on Sunday and a letter behind it in the same week would be two things from this yard at one door, and a man who cannot read is not a channel. Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 276 — *The Twenty-Sixth Week, a Morning Lost to the Body, and Three Doors on a Front Street*

**Tuesday the twentieth of April. The shed, and the front street of that harbor.** The wage Tuesday: the twenty-sixth week falls due, the column does not move, and twenty-six weeks have now fallen due with no envelope. **The holder of that writ loses the morning to the body**, stands at the bench outside the shed until about one with a cup of water and no more, and the day carries no figure because there is no figure for a day. In the afternoon he walks nine feet of chain and a bar of square iron down the front street and offers them at three doors, and **all three are refused**: a chandler, who would not take it at nothing and would not take it at four shillings and says there is a difference and he does not care which one you are trying to make; **a boat's owner of about fifty-five, who has been a hand short since the middle of July past, who asks whose it is, is told it came up out of a cut off the south end on the ninth of April past at forty fathoms and is not that yard's, and who then says out loud on his own step that the next man who comes down that street with a thing off a wreck will be able to say that this yard asks, and that a cat is not even the next worst thing; and a slop-chest and a counter in a shed off the back of that market, whose man of about sixty-one OFFERS A SHILLING FOR IT AND IS REFUSED**, and who asks for the reason and is told that a shilling is money, and that the day that thing is a transaction this yard is trading in a thing it does not own and cannot say whose it is, and that he would rather carry it about the harbor for the rest of his life than have one shilling of it in that book, **because a nail is a thing that cannot be a sale.** What the afternoon cost is not a money figure and has no conversion: **about nine people in a street now know that this yard has nine feet of chain it is trying to get rid of.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out, and a day.

### Chapter 277 — *The Outer Marks, a Bar That Is Only a Change in the Water, and Two Marks Two Shillings and Eightpence of Nothing*

**Wednesday the twenty-first of April. A mile and a quarter off the head of that mole, and the shed.** The price is named on Tuesday evening and not on Wednesday morning, being the boat of the man of the second lay for a day at one mark and eightpence and **Fen Barrow, twenty-six, hired by the day at a mark and two shillings**, the figure this yard has paid a hand by the day since the seventh of April last. **The bar is read out loud at the boat before a man puts a foot in her, as it has been since the seventh, and the six conditions are not read because there is no first entry and nothing to enter.** Nobody is in the water, nobody is on a lead, and no pot, gear or compressor of that yard is in the sea at all. The outer marks are two baulks of green timber with a ring of stone and a can of sheet iron on a stake, and **three bearings agree with a chart of about thirty years standing in a boat's locker to within a cable's length. Then the man of the second lay stands up and puts his hand on the gunwale at a change in the water: the bar is not a fence and there is no post in it — it is a line of about a mile and a half running north-north-west about four hundred yards beyond the outer marks, on which the water darkens and stands up in a low sea, and any man in a boat can see it and any man on a mole cannot.** Nobody and nothing of that yard is inside it. **What the yard bought with three hundred and fifty-two pence is the fact that nothing on that ground has changed since a man who has been dead about thirty years drew it**, and the man of the second lay says so at the steps, and that a man of this yard has now got the only instrument in that harbor that says the same thing twice. Cost: 352p out.

### Chapter 278 — *The Fortieth Charge, a Question in a Fish Market, and a Woman of Fifty-Eight With a Drawer Under Her Bed*

**Thursday the twenty-second of April. The fish market on that quay, and the shed.** The cistern is charged and walked on none, being the fortieth. **No person of that yard is in that market and nobody is sent, and the reason is in the second column: a yard which puts a woman of no office into a market has made her an instrument, and she is not one and has said so twice.** She goes on her own account. **A woman of about fifty-eight who has sold fish off that quay for thirty-one years, the end stall in the second row, four boxes of the small sort, a son in a city, has a sheet of paper with a seal on it in a drawer under her bed with her will and her marriage lines, and a man who buys fish from her told her in February to ask a woman of no office who has been in an office.** And in about forty people's hearing she asks the only question she wants answered: **is a person allowed to burn a thing like that.** And she is answered, in a fish market, in the words of a method, with no glass and no bench and no oath in the room. **What it cost is a figure whether there is one for it or not: a certificate of method in the reading of worked glass and its faults, held by a woman of thirty of no office, whose whole standing is that a person may repeat what she has said under that method where a thing is on a table and every person there may act on it. A method's words said in a market, about a thing that is not glass, in forty people's hearing, are a story told by a woman who used to be certified. The certificate is not withdrawn and cannot be withdrawn, because she is of no office and the office that keeps the copy is a building with a door on it and no person behind the counter, and the sheet she was issued is in a drawer in a town four days up this coast in somebody else's keeping whom this yard will not write to. And what she said was true, and nobody in that market was able to tell whether it was true or not, and that is the whole of the price.** And the price of the volume's own answer is a woman of about fifty-eight in a town where this yard has never put a question to anybody, and the man of the second lay says out loud that they are not going to write down what the market said and are going to write down that they did not go. Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 279 — *A Letter of One Page Up the Hill, Three Dates Kept Apart, and Three Marks That Are Not Going to Be Paid*

**Friday the twenty-third of April. The shed.** A letter of one page comes up the hill in the ordinary post, in an envelope that has been opened and closed once, in a young hand, signed with two names — **Wend Brace, thirty, and Ilse Brace, nineteen** — at the address this yard wrote to on the third of April. It asks for nothing and says three things, **and the three dates are kept apart and are not made into one**: the sheet was on a step in that town on **Tuesday the twentieth** and was not there in the morning; the man came in from the water at about half past five on **Wednesday the twenty-first** and found it where it had lain since the Tuesday, wet and dried, and stood in his own doorway and did not pick it up for about ten minutes, and was by that out of a day's work, being a hand hired by the day **at three marks**, and the boat's man is not in the letter and does not know there is a letter; and on **Thursday the twenty-second** his daughter read it out to him and he had it read twice and he cannot read and the letter says so in those words. **The three marks is a figure the boat's man pays and not this yard, and the boat's man is not named, and it goes in the second column and not the first, because the day after that somebody is going to say to an office that this yard has taken a man's wage off him.** And the refusal: **nobody is sent down the coast, no money is sent, and the three marks are not going to be, and the reason is not that this yard has not got them — it is that the day this yard pays that man three marks he is a man this yard has paid, and a man this yard has paid is a man this yard can be asked about, and that this yard put a page in his daughter's mouth in the autumn without asking him and has an arrangement with nothing.** And the woman of no office says the one thing none of the two of them had got to: **a man who cannot read who has had a piece of paper put on his step does not lose a day, he loses the four hundred years of being a person who is spoken to, and a house is the only kind of thing in this country you cannot refuse anybody anything about, and she has been trying to tell that to somebody all week and there has not been anybody in this country to tell.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out, and a man of thirty's afternoon, a man's day at three marks, and a season's peace.

### Chapter 280 — *Twelve Entries and Forty-Eight Lines and the Whole of What It Cost, Read Back From a Leaf With Four Columns*

**Saturday the twenty-fourth of April. The shed, and the whole standing inventory once.** The far bench is occupied from ten until about four and is not approached. **The leaf is read back in full, all four columns including the empty ones: twelve entries in the first column at the read-back, being three on the fourteenth of April past and nine since, one for every day from the fifteenth to the twenty-third and each of the nine named, and thirteen at the close with that day's own entry in it; forty-eight lines in the second, being seven on the fourteenth, thirty-two from the fifteenth to the twenty-third and nine this afternoon; none in the third, and a boy's hand of twenty-two at the head of it and the four lines above his named line still four, still nameless, not struck out, and he was not on the stone on any of the ten days; and eleven in the fourth, being the lines of the fourteenth of May and of the fifth, seventh, eleventh, thirteenth and fourteenth of April, and of the fifteenth, eighteenth, twenty-first, twenty-second and this afternoon.** And the shipwright says out loud that **five of the forty-eight are refusals, being the not-writing-back, the not-asking, the not-going, the not-sending and the not-paying, and that in about four years a man in a city reading that book will say that that yard never did anything, and that it will be the wrong shape of right, and that there is no column in that book for the saying of it.** And then the turn, said once, and entered without addition: **a page in a drawer in a house is custody that an office can reach; this yard built that on the sixth of March and has had it the wrong way round, because a house is not a place a thing is safe in, it is a place a person cannot refuse anybody anything about, and a drawer is a house and a yard is a house and this shed is a house; the four people four days down that coast wrote four lines about a record and this yard leaned on them as if they were about a house for forty-two days; and the price of that is now a man of thirty who cannot read who lost a day of three marks, and a woman of about fifty-eight in this harbor with a will and a marriage lines and a sealed paper in a drawer under her bed, and a girl of nineteen who read a page out in a kitchen in the autumn — and both of those are ours, and neither of them is money, and we are not going to see either of them again unless we write, and we are not going to write.** And then, for Monday: the wall, the cistern, and then on the Tuesday after that the twenty-seventh week, nineteen marks four shillings and ninepence, a chain on a nail, a mole with a foot at forty fathoms, no instrument, no money, nobody to ask — **and a woman in this harbor who has told forty people in a market that a keeper is a function, and about sixty boats on this coast with a sheet in them that says where every other copy is, and in about two months those two things are going to meet in a place where neither of them is us.** Cost: the whole batch, itemised, and nothing paid twice.

### The batch's money, re-derived from the ten chapters and closing once

**3,931 + 0 − 834 = 3,097.** The bag opens at **twenty-four marks, seven shillings and sevenpence, being three thousand nine hundred and thirty-one pence**, and closes at **nineteen marks, four shillings and ninepence, being three thousand and ninety-seven pence**. **NOTHING CAME INTO THAT YARD ON ANY OF THE TEN DAYS, and the whole of what has come into that yard since the sixth of December past is one thousand seven hundred and sixty pence, being the eleven marks of the sixth of April last, and there is nothing else.** Out, in the ten days, and it is four things: **one penny at a box on the corner of that mole on the fifteenth**; **four hundred and eighty pence on the seventeenth**, being a day at the face of the second seam on the hirers' own account with nobody in the water; **one penny at the same box on the eighteenth**, being a form of an office this yard does not belong to; and **three hundred and fifty-two pence on the twenty-first**, being the boat of the man of the second lay for a day at a mark and eightpence and a man of twenty-six hired by the day at a mark and two shillings, the price named on the Tuesday evening. **That is eight hundred and thirty-four pence, being five marks, two shillings and tenpence, and with the fifty-nine marks, twelve shillings and twopence that had gone out since the sixth of December it is sixty-five marks, one shilling and eightpence in twenty-one transactions at seven counters, which is ten thousand four hundred and twenty pence.** The running total on the page at each close is **fifty-nine marks, twelve shillings and threepence in eighteen at seven** after the fifteenth and the sixteenth, **sixty-two marks, twelve shillings and threepence in nineteen at seven** after the seventeenth, **sixty-two marks, twelve shillings and fourpence in twenty at seven** after the eighteenth, and the figure above from the twenty-first. **The figure the bag is short of the wage of four marks DOES NOT EXIST and did not exist on any of these ten days and will not come back while that bag is above six hundred and forty pence, and nobody in that shed said one word about it at any hour of any of them. A MARK IS A HUNDRED AND SIXTY PENCE AND A SHILLING IS TWELVE IN THIS BOOK, and a day of a hired hand is three marks, and every figure above is derivable from those rates.**

### The batch's costs, in the order they were spent, and none of them paid twice

1. **A penny of post on the fifteenth** — four lines to a woman of no office, and the undertaking of the fourteenth broken to get it sent.
2. **An undertaking broken on the page.** *We are not going to be the reason a person in a town two days down that coast is frightened in her own kitchen*, said on the fourteenth of April, and a form put at that address on the eighteenth. Entered as **broken and not as kept**, and the reason given is about a drawer and not about a person.
3. **Four hundred and eighty pence on the seventeenth** — a day at the face of the second seam, not booked and not bookable.
4. **A penny of post on the eighteenth** — the form, and the whole of what that act cost, which is in items 5, 6 and 7 and nowhere else.
5. **A person's afternoon, on the twentieth, in a town two days down that coast.** A man stood in his own doorway for about ten minutes and did not pick up a sheet off his step.
6. **A person's day of work, on the twenty-first, being three marks**, paid to a boat's man this yard does not know, for a man this yard has never spoken to, and **not paid, not sent, and not going to be**, and the reason entered.
7. **A season's peace** — a girl of nineteen who read a page out in that kitchen in the autumn and has now read a second thing, and a mother who has a drawer under her bed she did not have a use for in February.
8. **A certificate of method, and the standing of a certificate in a market.** Spent on the twenty-second in about forty people's hearing, and it cannot be withdrawn, and there is no office to take it from.
9. **Three hundred and fifty-two pence on the twenty-first** — a boat and a man, to be told that nothing on that ground has changed.
10. **A day to the body on the twentieth**, which carries no figure because there is no figure for a day.
11. **Standing.** About nine people on a front street now know that this yard has a chain it is trying to get rid of, and a man of about fifty-five of that harbor has said out loud what that does to the next man who comes down it.

### The volume's shape after thirty chapters of it

**FORTY CONSECUTIVE DAYS FROM TUESDAY THE SIXTEENTH OF MARCH TO SATURDAY THE TWENTY-FOURTH OF APRIL IN THIRTY CHAPTERS, THE FIRST TEN OF THEM TWO DAYS TO A CHAPTER AND THE LAST TWENTY ONE DAY TO A CHAPTER, NO DAY MISSING AND NO DAY IN TWO CHAPTERS, NO CHAPTER STATES A YEAR, AND THREE DAYS ON WHICH A PERSON OF THAT YARD WENT INTO THE WATER, ALL THREE OF THEM OFF THE SOUTH END, ON THE SEAWARD SIDE OF THE BAR OF FOUR HUNDRED YARDS OFF THE OUTER MARK AND NOT INSIDE IT. MOVEMENTS 1, 2 AND 3 ARE WRITTEN. THE VOLUME'S QUESTION IS KNOWN TO BE ANSWERABLE AND THIS PARTY IS KNOWN TO BE THE WRONG PARTY FOR IT. A FORM HAS NOW BEEN SERVED BY BEING PUT IN THE POST AT AN ADDRESS, AND THE PRICE OF THAT IS A MAN'S AFTERNOON AND A MAN'S DAY AND A SEASON'S PEACE, AND THE PRICE OF THE VOLUME'S OWN SOLUTION IS A MAN OF THIRTY WHO CANNOT READ AND A WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT WITH A DRAWER UNDER HER BED AND A GIRL OF NINETEEN WHO READ A PAGE OUT IN A KITCHEN. THE BATCH'S CLOSING POSITION IS THAT A WORKING HARBOR IS POORER, THAT A YARD HAS SPENT FIVE MARKS, TWO SHILLINGS AND TENPENCE ON TEN DAYS AND EARNED NOTHING, THAT IT IS CARRYING A CHAIN ON A NAIL IT CANNOT SELL AND A BAR IN A DRAWER IT CANNOT GIVE AWAY, THAT IT HAS AN ADDRESS IN A TOWN TWO DAYS DOWN THAT COAST IT DID NOT WANT, AND THAT IN ABOUT TWO MONTHS A WORD SAID IN A FISH MARKET AND A SHEET IN ABOUT SIXTY BOATS ARE GOING TO MEET IN A PLACE WHERE NEITHER OF THEM IS IT.**

## Volume 06, Batch 0004 (Chapters 281–290) — Movement 4, a room under seventy fathoms, and a record with nine hands' worth of disagreement in it. **THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT IS PAID. THE VOLUME IS NOT CLOSED.**

**WRITTEN 28 SEPTEMBER 2026 OUT OF `outline/volume-06.md` § *Escalation sequence* MOVEMENT 4, ITS LOCKS L1, L1a, L2, L5, L6, L7, L8, L15, L16, L20, L21, L22, L23, L25, L26, L27, L28, L28a, L29, L31, L32, L33 AND L34, AND THE FOUR STATE FILES AS THEY STOOD AFTER BATCH 0003. NO CARD FILE EXISTS FOR THIS BATCH AND NONE WAS INVENTED; `outline/batches/` STILL HOLDS `volume-06-batch-0001.md` AND NOTHING ELSE FOR VOLUME 06. REPAIRED THE SAME DAY AGAINST `logs/batch-0004.review.log`, WHICH RETURNED `NOVEL_REVIEW_RESULT: FIX`; THE REPAIR RESTARTED NOTHING, REWROTE NO CHAPTER AND CHANGED NO PLOT, AND ITS WHOLE DISPOSITION IS AT `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0004 (Volume 06) repairs*. 26,455 WORDS ACROSS TEN CHAPTERS** (Ch281 2,234 · Ch282 2,512 · Ch283 2,482 · Ch284 2,569 · Ch285 3,049 · Ch286 2,608 · Ch287 2,593 · Ch288 2,254 · Ch289 2,195 · Ch290 3,959), measured with `wc -w` including headings, against the 2,200–3,200 guideline; the pre-repair figures of 26,065 and 3,787 are withdrawn. **NINE CHAPTERS ARE INSIDE IT AND ONE IS PAST IT, and the one is the batch's close, which was written at 4,944 and cut by 857 on three passes and has since grown by 172, and which carries the read-back of nine days, the three statements, the turn and the whole standing inventory once, as `chapter-0260.md`, `chapter-0270.md` and `chapter-0280.md` carried it at 4,363, 3,885 and 4,679.** The apparatus is **18.9%** of the batch's words on the stated method, being 4,990 of 26,455, and Batch 0003's ten chapters measure 15.2% on the same method and not the 40.6% this file previously carried for it, the two figures wearing one name on two definitions. It is thinned on purpose and now for the reason the batch's own dispatch asked for: **the whole standing inventory is carried in Chapter 290 and nowhere else, and seven chapters that had been repeating it word for word now close on dated work.**

**THE GRID IS FIXED BY `outline/volume-06.md` LOCK L1 AND NOT BY AN ALMANAC: the fifteenth of March next is a Monday, and this batch takes the same shape Batches 0002 and 0003 took — ten chapters, ten consecutive days, ONE DAY TO A CHAPTER, no day missing, no day in two chapters, and no chapter states a year. THE BATCH RUNS SUNDAY THE TWENTY-FIFTH OF APRIL TO TUESDAY THE FOURTH OF MAY, and the departure from lock L1 item 5 is the same recorded departure and is not re-argued here.** Three cistern rota days fall in it, being the **twenty-sixth and twenty-ninth of April and the third of May**, and were walked on none of them, being **the forty-first, forty-second and forty-third**. Two wall wipes fall in it, being the **fifty-first on Monday the twenty-sixth and the fifty-second on Monday the third of May**; the fifty-third is on Monday the tenth of May and falls outside. **TWO wage Tuesdays fall in it, being the twenty-seventh and the fourth, the column did not move on either, and the wage list therefore stands at TWENTY-EIGHT weeks fallen due at the close of this batch; the hand-off that dispatched this batch counted one and the chapters followed the anchor, and the working is at `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0004 as written*, item 1.4.**

### Chapter 281 — *The Word Is Named Once in a Shed and Goes in No Column, and a Counter Off the Fish Market Is Decided On*

**Sunday the twenty-fifth of April. The shed on the mole.** Nothing in, nothing out, the far bench not occupied at any hour. **MARA VENN, A PERSON OF NO OFFICE OF THIS HARBOR, THIRTY, NAMES THE WORD *QUIETENING*, AND SHE NAMES IT ONCE, AND IT IS THE ONLY TIME THE WORD IS SPOKEN IN THE WHOLE OF VOLUME 06 UP TO THIS CHAPTER.** It is a name for a practice and not for a person, a place, an office or a title: two people bring what they have each read out of the same piece of work, the two readings cannot both be true, and the office requires one of them to be asked in writing, in a room with a second person in it, **to withdraw the reading and not to change it.** She filled in the forms and asked the questions for nine years. **She says out loud that nobody she ever asked said who wanted it, not one in nine years, and that she stopped asking in about the fifth year, and that the yard is not to fill that gap, and she is right.** It goes in no column of that yard's own book and on no wall in that harbor, and the fourth column takes its twelfth line. And the decision of the day: **he is going down that hill on Monday to the copyist's counter off the fish market, because it is the only counter in this country that copies anything without asking what it is for, and he does not know what sheet he is going to buy, and the reason against the line of the going is that he has had no better plan since the sixth of March.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 282 — *The Fifty-First Time That Wall Came Down, the Forty-First Charge Walked on None, and a Woman of Fifty-Eight Who Does Not Read What She Copies*

**Monday the twenty-sixth of April. A room off the fish market, and the shed.** The wall comes down bare for the fifty-first time and the cistern is charged and walked on none, being the forty-first, and the far bench is not occupied. **PERNEL SAYE, FIFTY-EIGHT, A COPYIST OF THIS HARBOR WHO HAS KEPT THAT COUNTER FOR THIRTY-ONE YEARS, HAS NO OFFICE ABOVE IT, IS NOT OF THIS PARTY, IS NOT ON A LADDER AND IS NOT A VILLAIN, TELLS HIM OF HER OWN ACCOUNT AND UNASKED that she has one sheet on the top shelf at the back of her room, made nine years ago for a man who was buying a house and wanted the oldest paper that came with it copied, and that he paid and did not come back.** The question he asked was where in this town a copy of a thing out of the drowned city would be, and he said out loud at her counter that he was not asking what was on one. **Her rule is the volume's argument in an ordinary person's mouth: she does not read what she copies, because the day a copyist finds out what a thing is she is a person who knows what it says, and a person who knows what a paper says is somebody's to ask, and a copyist cannot have a counter on a street and know what is on the shelf behind her own head.** She asks one thing for herself and he gives it: **if you take it off the shelf, do not come back and tell me what is on it.** **He asks her nothing else and he does not buy it on that day, and both refusals are in the second column with reasons, and the reason he did not buy is not money.** And the sentence the whole batch stands on, said by the ship's hand: **a set is a pressure house's set on a book and it goes where the book sends it, and there is no book on that coast that sends it four hundred miles west, and there is no yard on that coast that rents one, and there is nothing in this country a man can hire that will go and stand in a room seventy fathoms down and hold a lamp in it; and we cannot go and we will not go are different words and only the first of them is true.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 283 — *The Twenty-Seventh Week With No Envelope, a Penny Off a Counter, and Fourteen Marks Three Shillings and Fourpence Named in a Shed Before Anybody Agreed to It*

**Tuesday the twenty-seventh of April. A room off the fish market, and the shed.** The wage Tuesday: a wage of four marks is not paid, no envelope is made, the column does not move, the twenty-seventh week falls due. **He buys the sheet at eight in the morning for one penny and she puts the penny in the box and does not look up, exactly as she said she would, and the transaction takes about eleven seconds.** The sheet is one sheet about sixteen inches by eleven of a heavy rag paper gone the color of a wet pipe, folded twice across and once down with the middle fold soft and parting at one corner, and **it is a copy**, in a hand nine years old, copied entire on both sides with no lines ruled and no blot, and at the head of the face there is a name of a chamber. The rest of the day is the price: **between ten and two the holder of that writ names out loud at the bench, before anybody has agreed to it, the cost of a descent for the following day — nine marks for the compressor, the housing, the pipe, the gauges and one man off a Crown house book, two marks and eightpence for the ship's hand's man, a mark and two shillings for a hand on the hose, the same three figures as the fifth of April in the same order — and a second man off that book at two marks and eightpence, making fourteen marks three shillings and fourpence, — the identical figure the eighth, ninth and tenth of April past came to, component for component, the same man off the same book on both days, and the reason a second man is on that book at all is not that the figure is higher and is that he cannot hold a slate and read a figure at the same time and is not going to find out which of the two he loses on the bottom.** Garrow Fisk has the set out of Ternwatch by six in the morning and the book will send the second man. The far bench is occupied from ten until four and is not approached. Cost: one penny out.

### Chapter 284 — *The Second Descent Off the South End at About Forty Fathoms, a Second Man Off a Book, and Eighty-Four Pounds on a Face of a Hundred and Fifty*

**Wednesday the twenty-eighth of April. A mile and a quarter off the south end of that mole, and the shed.** Two thousand two hundred and eighty pence out. **The six conditions of a first entry are read aloud at the boat in order, there is no first entry, there is no harbor authority's book to go in, and this yard's own book is not that book; the bar of four hundred yards off the outer marks is named out loud at the boat, the ground is off the south end and round the corner from it, and no person, no boat and no lead of that yard is inside the bar on that day or any day, and the bar has not been narrowed by anything and was not asked to be.** The pot goes down at about twenty past nine and comes up at about ten to twelve. **The gauge is a pressure gauge on a face of a hundred and fifty and reads eighty-four pounds, as it read on the eighth, ninth and tenth of April past, and the pencil mark where the needle stood on the tenth is not moved, and that is the only reason the two books are the same book.** The pot is landed on the top course, in the empty socket the bar of square iron came out of on the ninth of April. **AND ITS LEAD WEIGHT COMES DOWN ACROSS THE BELLY OF A HERRINGMAN'S NET, AND ABOUT SIX FATHOMS OF THAT NET IS NOW FLAT ON A COURSE OF CUT STONE UNDER A WEIGHT AND IS NOT CUT AND IS NOT MENDED AND WILL NOT COME UP WHOLE**, and nobody in that boat cut it, and the ground is that man's by first refusal, and this yard was on it without him and had named the price the day before and had done all of it right. **The finding is a join: the figure at the near end of the top course is the forty-second and is cut about a quarter of an inch deep where the other forty-one are cut about an inch, and its strokes are laid cross-stroke first, and that says the stone was made in two hands at two times and says nothing whatever about which of them is right, or why, or by whom.** The holder of that writ said out loud that it was to be written down as what it is and not as what it means, **and he could not hold the slate and gave it across the thwart at about four o'clock, and the ship's hand's man wrote the entry.**

### Chapter 285 — *The Forty-Second Charge Walked on None, and a Copy Read Under a Lamp by Two People and Not by the Third*

**Thursday the twenty-ninth of April. The shed on that mole.** The cistern is charged and walked on none, being the forty-second. **AT ABOUT TEN AT NIGHT, WITH THE LAMP LIT AND TURNED ROUND AND THE DOOR SHUT AND THE MARKET OFF THE BOARDS, THE HOLDER OF THAT WRIT AND THE SHIP'S HAND READ A COPY OF A MUNICIPAL SHEET OF A CHAMBER OF A DROWNED CITY. THERE WERE TWO OF THEM IN THAT ROOM AND NOBODY ELSE. THE THIRD PERSON IS NOT IN THE ROOM AND IS THE ONE WHO MOST WANTS TO BE:** a woman of no office was asked on the Wednesday evening, said yes before he had got to the end of the sentence, and was then told she was not to come, and gave the second half of the reason herself — **she was not being left out because she was not able, she was being left out because she is the only one of the three who could say whether it is true, and if she said it then in about four years a man in a city would write *she said so* on the back of a sheet.** **The order is nine lines and there is no tenth: the fleet is ordered down; the order is of the fleet and not of a master of it; the masters are not to be asked which of them made it; the order is carried by the masters and not by any office; no copy of it is to be made, and a copy of it is not it, and a man holding a copy has not got an order; and it is to be carried out in one tide. THE FOOT OF THE SHEET CARRIES NINE MARKS — SIX OF A CIRCLE WITH ONE STROKE, SAME SIZE, SAME HAND; TWO OF A CIRCLE WITH TWO STROKES, SMALLER, OFF THE CENTER, NOT EVEN WITH EACH OTHER; AND A NINTH OF NINE SHORT LINES, CUT AND NOT STRUCK, A THIRD OF THE DEPTH OF THE OTHERS, AT AN ANGLE NONE OF THE OTHERS IS AT — AND UNDER THEM, IN THE SHEET'S OWN HAND, ONE LINE SAYING THE NINTH IS NOT ONE OF OURS AND IS NOT TO BE COUNTED WITH THE NINE AND IS NOT TO BE ASKED ABOUT. THE TWO MARKS DO NOT AGREE AND THE DISAGREEMENT IS THE FINDING AND IS NOT A DEFECT IN THE EVIDENCE.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 286 — *The Thirtieth of April, a Far Bench Occupied From Ten Until Four, and a Disagreement About Which Column a Penny Belongs In*

**Friday the thirtieth of April. The shed on the mole.** The far bench is occupied from ten until four and is not approached. **He wants the sheet in the first column and the ship's hand will not have it, and a person of no office says what both of them are actually arguing about: the first column is what a man can go and look at, and no man alive can go and look at that, and it is exactly the size of the truth, and the big thing is on a shelf in a room off a fish market.** And then the turn, out loud: **the thing this yard has been calling an office's fear was real, it happened to real people in real water, it was not one man's vanity and it was not a lie and it was not a weapon, and it was ordered by more than one of them, together, and they argued about it afterwards and left the argument on the paper — AND KNOWING THAT DOES NOT MAKE ANYBODY SAFE, and this yard has five marks in a bag and twenty-eight weeks of no envelope and a form on a step two days down that coast, and now it knows the drawer matters, which is worse.** And the other half: **the sheet is incomplete, and a party that says false about a record that is incomplete has stopped being the party that says what it is not, and the office's three hundred years is not withdrawn and not softened.** And the hole, which stays: **we are not going to know who wanted it, the reason against the finding is the reason of the second of April last about a trade and a price, no letter is to be written to any town and no man sent to any town, and a second-column fact with no name in it is not a document.** The whole of the sheet goes in the second column with a reason against every line. Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 287 — *Saturday the First of May, Six Fathoms of a Herringman's Net Told on the Mole Steps, and Six Doors and a Seventh One That Is a Drawer*

**Saturday the first of May. The mole steps, and the shed.** **ELIAS VOKE, FIFTY-ONE, OF THE HERRING BOAT OF THE SECOND LAY, IS TOLD ON THE STEPS AT ABOUT HALF PAST TEN IN ABOUT NINE PEOPLE'S HEARING that a pot was landed on his ground on the Wednesday at about forty fathoms and that its lead came down on the belly of his net, and that about six fathoms of it is now flat on a course of cut stone and will not come up whole. HE SAYS THE FIGURE IS SIX AND NOT ABOUT SIX, because about is what a man writes when he wants to be argued with, and that entry is six and not about six. He asks for nothing, is given nothing, and says he would rather be a man this yard has not paid, and that not being thanked is the arrangement. The reason this yard did not ask him on the Tuesday is the standing reason about a man this yard has paid, and it was not improved on, and he says out loud that the same sentence is going round forty people and nine steps and that this yard does not have a speech, it has a sentence.** Then the doors, and six are ruled out — the tin, a wall, the ordinary post, the four people of that coast, the copyist's counter, and a fire, and the burning is the keeping and it is the same door — **and the seventh place is a drawer in that shed, and a drawer is a house, and this yard said all of that out loud on the twenty-fourth of April past and meant it, and on the twenty-seventh it went down the hill and put a penny on a counter.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out, and six fathoms of another man's gear.

### Chapter 288 — *Sunday the Second of May, Two Instruments and Two Hands, a Morning That Carries No Figure, and Nine Short Lines That This Yard Is Not Going to Count*

**Sunday the second of May. The crown of that mole, and the shed.** **HE IS ON THE CROWN FROM ABOUT HALF PAST SEVEN UNTIL ABOUT ONE WITH A COMPASS IN HIS HAND AND HE CANNOT USE IT, and at ten past eight he says the only sentence anybody in that harbor has ever used for it, and nobody asks him about it, and the morning goes in the second column with a reason against the line and no name in it and carries no figure because there is no figure for a morning.** The entry of the figure in the top course is written in the ship's left hand from the slate, and **he does not touch the slate and does not check the entry.** And then the temptation, and the refusal: **the ninth mark on the copy is the same shape as the nine-line mark already in that yard's own book, and the two are not to be counted together, and this yard refuses out loud to go to a pressure house at Ternwatch and ask a man there whether his foreman cut cross-stroke first, to write to a town four days up that coast, and to put the two of them in one line together, and the reason against all four lines is that a shape is not a thing and nobody in this country can go and look at either of them.** The chain is on a nail and the bar is in the drawer and neither can be sold nor given away, **and this yard went back down to the socket the bar came out of on the Wednesday and was not able to say one word about whose the bar is.** Cost: a day, and no figure.

### Chapter 289 — *The Fifty-Second Time That Wall Came Down, the Forty-Third Charge Walked on None, and a Question Asked at a Mole Head and Not Answered*

**Monday the third of May. The mole steps, and the shed.** The wall comes down bare for the fifty-second time and the cistern is charged and walked on none, being the forty-third, and the far bench is occupied from ten until four and is not approached. **It is decided in that shed that no part of the sheet is to be said out loud in about forty people, and the reason is standing about thirty feet away: a certificate of method in the reading of worked glass and its faults has already had the standing of it spent in that fish market in about forty people's hearing on the twenty-second of April past, it cannot be given back because there is no office in this country to take it from her, and that price is not being paid a second time because the sheet is better.** And then **a person of no office asks a question out loud at a mole head in about nine people's hearing and says in advance that there is no person in it and wanted it written down that there is no person in it: who was the first one to use a mark like that to keep a thing in an emergency instead of holding it. NOBODY ANSWERS. The holder of that writ says he does not know and that there is nobody in this country he could ask and that he is not going to write to a town to find out. The question goes in no column of that yard's own book, is on no wall in that harbor, and carries no figure.** And the fourth column's seventeenth line takes it, and the batch enters the convergence — about sixty boats on this coast with a sheet that says where every other copy is, and a word in a market, and about seven weeks, and this yard is not going to be the one that moves them along. Cost: nothing in and nothing out.

### Chapter 290 — *Tuesday the Fourth of May, the Twenty-Eighth Week With No Envelope, and Twenty-Two Entries and Eighty-Eight Lines Read Back From a Leaf With Four Columns*

**Tuesday the fourth of May. The shed on the mole, and the bench outside it.** **The wage Tuesday: the column does not move and twenty-eight weeks have now fallen due with no envelope, the second wage Tuesday in these ten days on which the column did not move.** The read-back at about four: **twenty-two entries in the first column at the hour of the read-back and twenty-three at the close, being thirteen before the batch, nine from the twenty-fifth of April to the third of May and one this evening; eighty-eight lines in the second, being forty-eight before and thirty-one in the nine days and nine this afternoon; none in the third, with the boy's hand at the head of it and the four nameless lines above his named line still four and still not struck out; and eighteen in the fourth, the last of them the last thing in that book.** The ship's hand says the one thing that went wrong: **nine refusals in ten days, four things that cost money, and one of them cost a man of fifty-one six fathoms of his gear, and there is still no column in that book for the saying of any of it.** **And then three people say three things and all three are right and they differ in what they are about and not in whether they are correct: the words, by the holder of that writ; the marks, by the ship's hand; the house, by the woman of no office — and hers is that none of the three of us is a keeper and all three of us are, and that is a chair, and the chair is in a house, and there is no handle on the inside of any of the three of them.** And then the turn, out loud a second time in ten days with the reason for the second time given first, entered in the first column in the words of the Friday, without addition, in two halves: **the office's fear was real and it does not make anybody safe, and the opacity was expensive for a reason, and a reason is not a warrant, and this yard does not get to be the one that says that office was right, and the sheet is incomplete.** Cost: nothing in and nothing out. **THE BAG CLOSES AT FIVE MARKS, ONE SHILLING AND FOURPENCE, BEING EIGHT HUNDRED AND SIXTEEN PENCE, and the whole of what went out in the ten days is two thousand two hundred and eighty-one pence, being a penny and a day's set.**

---

**27,571 WORDS ACROSS TEN CHAPTERS AFTER THE REPAIRS OF 28 SEPTEMBER 2026, AN AVERAGE OF 2,757, AGAINST 2,200–3,200 A CHAPTER: Ch291 3,571 · Ch292 2,782 · Ch293 2,368 · Ch294 2,279 · Ch295 2,286 · Ch296 2,233 · Ch297 2,651 · Ch298 2,415 · Ch299 2,983 · Ch300 4,003, MEASURED WITH `wc -w` INCLUDING HEADINGS. THE FIGURES *27,706*, *27,212*, *2,771*, *2,721*, *3,259*, *3,263*, *2,766*, *2,284*, *2,292*, *2,248*, *2,242*, *2,379*, *2,604*, *2,414*, *2,990*, *3,997*, *4,527*, *27,536* AND *4,403* ARE WITHDRAWN, ALL OF THEM BEING EARLIER MEASUREMENTS; the full disposition is at `state/continuity.md` § *Batch 0005 (Volume 06) repairs* and § *Batch 0005 (Volume 06) review repairs* and again below.**
